How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** labels for specific crop types (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) per field or pixel.
- **R2 (must):** ready-made multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data already paired with those labels, so you don't build a pipeline.
- **R3 (must):** a licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- I assumed you want something you can download now, not something behind an application.

## Shortlist
Every dataset here is a direct download with no account or application. The registry record confirmed the licence on 2026-11-11, except where the table says otherwise.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | PASTIS (France, parcel patches) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 28.8 GB | Strong | Today |
| 2 | EuroCropsML (Europe, per-parcel series) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Not recorded | Strong | Today |
| 3 | BreizhCrops (Brittany, about 600k parcels) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Strong | Partly: the registry lookup failed, so the licence comes from the search record |
| 4 | TimeSen2Crop (Austria, 16 crops, pixels) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | about 1 GB | Strong | Today |
| 5 | AgriSen-COG (AT, BE, ES, DK, NL) | Direct download; check which parts are on Zenodo | CC-BY-4.0 | Large | Strong | Today (label part) |
| 6 | Sen4AgriNet, TACO repackaging (Catalonia and France) | Direct download from Hugging Face | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 10,013 patches | Strong | Today |
| 7 | CropHarvest (global) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |
| 8 | Brandenburg-S12-CropVal | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |
| 9 | Peru multi-annual S2 crop dataset (2026) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |
| 10 | IBM/NASA multi-temporal crop classification (US) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |

## Details

**1. PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942)
- It has parcel-level Sentinel-2 time series patches for France, with crop-type semantic and instance labels. It's the most used benchmark of this kind, with about 93k Zenodo downloads.
- From memory, not confirmed: it has 18 crop classes, and a companion repository holds the code and splits.
- Download the 28.8 GB zip from the record page.
- It is in patch (segmentation) format rather than pixel tables, so you may need to convert it for pixel-wise or parcel-wise models.

**2. EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445)
- Per-parcel median Sentinel-2 L1C time series with 176 crop classes and 706,683 labelled points. Both figures come from the paper abstract. It was built for few-shot transfer across countries.
- It is CC-BY-SA, so derivatives must stay share-alike. Whether a trained model counts as a derivative is legally unsettled, so ask your supervisor or library.
- The data are per-parcel medians, so there is no spatial context.
- Papers that used it:
  - The dataset paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7
  - A 2026 baselines paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117

**3. BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341)
- Parcels in Brittany with both top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere Sentinel-2 series. The paper abstract says reference models, pretrained weights and a GitHub repository (dl4sits/breizhcrops) come with it.
- From memory: one region and one year (2017).
- The dataset paper compares seven deep networks and a random forest baseline, so you get ready comparisons: https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020

**4. TimeSen2Crop** (https://zenodo.org/records/4715631)
- More than 1 million pixel samples for 16 crop types, September 2017 to August 2018. Atmospherically corrected, with cloud, snow and shadow flags.
- The download is about 1 GB, and it has about 2.8k Zenodo downloads. This is the easiest one to start with.
- From memory: the area is Austria.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965

**5. AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012)
- It has Sentinel-2 cloud-optimised GeoTIFFs for five countries, labelled from farmer declarations (LPIS).
- The Zenodo record I verified holds the label part. Check the paper for where the imagery lives.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3390/rs15122980

**6. Sen4AgriNet, TACO repackaging** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco)
- It has 10,013 Sentinel-2 patches over Catalonia and France for 2019–2020, with 168 harmonised crop and land-cover classes.
- This is a third-party repackaging with a split the repackager constructed, not the original authors' release. The card says to follow the original licence, and I haven't checked that one.
- Original paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2022.3164771

**7. CropHarvest** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170)
- Global labels with time series. From memory, most labels are crop versus non-crop, with a multiclass subset. Check how many specific-crop labels you get before committing.

**8–10. Brandenburg-S12-CropVal, Peru, IBM/NASA**
- Brandenburg-S12-CropVal (https://zenodo.org/records/18632974):
  - It is a Sentinel-1/2 cropland segmentation set for cross-regional transfer.
  - I haven't read its class list, so I don't know whether the labels are specific crops.
- Peru (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051):
  - Multi-annual Sentinel-2 crop type data, new in 2026, with no papers using it yet.
  - I haven't read its class list.
- IBM/NASA (https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):
  - This is a US dataset built from HLS imagery, which is a 30 m Landsat–Sentinel-2 composite, not native 10 m Sentinel-2.
  - From memory, it has 13 classes.

**On licences (R3).** All the licences above allow publishing results. Plain CC-BY (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, TimeSen2Crop, AgriSen-COG, and the Brandenburg, Peru and IBM/NASA sets) is the safest for sharing trained models. The CC-BY-SA datasets (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet, CropHarvest) are fine for results. For released models, check how your university treats share-alike. If you want no ambiguity, start with 1, 3 or 4.

## Known, not confirmed
- **ZueriCrop:** the Hugging Face copy (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isaaccorley/zuericrop) is CC-BY-NC-4.0. I didn't check the original release or licence. A non-commercial licence may complicate sharing models.
- **Original Sen4AgriNet release (GitHub):** I didn't check its home page or licence.
- **S2Agri / PixelSet:** Hugging Face copies exist under monster-monash (CC-BY-4.0), but I didn't check the original home or access terms.
- **DENETHOR (Germany):** I searched for it but didn't confirm a record, so I can't say where it lives now.

## Not recommended, and why
- **EuroCrops:** parcel labels only, with no imagery. EuroCropsML is the version paired with Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** county-level yield prediction, not field or pixel crop type.
- **Hugging Face ZueriCrop copy:** non-commercial licence, as noted above.

## What I could not find
- Zenodo and DataCite failed intermittently during the search, so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't confirm the sizes of several entries, and I didn't get class lists for the three partial-fit datasets.
- Everything marked "from memory" above was not confirmed today.

## Next step
Download TimeSen2Crop (about 1 GB) and BreizhCrops first and get a baseline running. Then add PASTIS or EuroCropsML if you want spatial context or cross-country transfer. Before you commit to a dataset, open the dataset page or paper to confirm the class list and licence, because these were checked on 2026-11-11 and can change.

The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-W4YHCE/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

| 5 | AgriSen-COG (AT, BE, ES, DK, NL) | Direct download; check which parts are on Zenodo | CC-BY-4.0 | Large | Strong | Today (label part) |
| 6 | Sen4AgriNet, TACO repackaging (Catalonia and France) | Direct download from Hugging Face | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 10,013 patches | Strong | Today |
| 7 | CropHarvest (global) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |
| 8 | Brandenburg-S12-CropVal | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |
| 9 | Peru multi-annual S2 crop dataset (2026) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |
| 10 | IBM/NASA multi-temporal crop classification (US) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Not recorded | Partial | Today |

## Details

**1. PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942)
- It has parcel-level Sentinel-2 time series patches for France, with crop-type semantic and instance labels. It's the most used benchmark of this kind, with about 93k Zenodo downloads.
- From memory, not confirmed: it has 18 crop classes, and a companion repository holds the code and splits.
- Download the 28.8 GB zip from the record page.
- It is in patch (segmentation) format rather than pixel tables, so you may need to convert it for pixel-wise or parcel-wise models.

**2. EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445)
- Per-parcel median Sentinel-2 L1C time series with 176 crop classes and 706,683 labelled points. Both figures come from the paper abstract. It was built for few-shot transfer across countries.
- It is CC-BY-SA, so derivatives must stay share-alike. Whether a trained model counts as a derivative is legally unsettled, so ask your supervisor or library.
- The data are per-parcel medians, so there is no spatial context.
- Papers that used it:
  - The dataset paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7
  - A 2026 baselines paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117

**3. BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341)
- Parcels in Brittany with both top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere Sentinel-2 series. The paper abstract says reference models, pretrained weights and a GitHub repository (dl4sits/breizhcrops) come with it.
- From memory: one region and one year (2017).
- The dataset paper compares seven deep networks and a random forest baseline, so you get ready comparisons: https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020

**4. TimeSen2Crop** (https://zenodo.org/records/4715631)
- More than 1 million pixel samples for 16 crop types, September 2017 to August 2018. Atmospherically corrected, with cloud, snow and shadow flags.
- The download is about 1 GB, and it has about 2.8k Zenodo downloads. This is the easiest one to start with.
- From memory: the area is Austria.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965

**5. AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012)
- It has Sentinel-2 cloud-optimised GeoTIFFs for five countries, labelled from farmer declarations (LPIS).
- The Zenodo record I verified holds the label part. Check the paper for where the imagery lives.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3390/rs15122980

**6. Sen4AgriNet, TACO repackaging** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco)
- It has 10,013 Sentinel-2 patches over Catalonia and France for 2019–2020, with 168 harmonised crop and land-cover classes.
- This is a third-party repackaging with a split the repackager constructed, not the original authors' release. The card says to follow the original licence, and I haven't checked that one.
- Original paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2022.3164771

**7. CropHarvest** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170)
- Global labels with time series. From memory, most labels are crop versus non-crop, with a multiclass subset. Check how many specific-crop labels you get before committing.

**8–10. Brandenburg-S12-CropVal, Peru, IBM/NASA**
- Brandenburg-S12-CropVal (https://zenodo.org/records/18632974):
  - It is a Sentinel-1/2 cropland segmentation set for cross-regional transfer.
  - I haven't read its class list, so I don't know whether the labels are specific crops.
- Peru (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051):
  - Multi-annual Sentinel-2 crop type data, new in 2026, with no papers using it yet.
  - I haven't read its class list.
- IBM/NASA (https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):
  - This is a US dataset built from HLS imagery, which is a 30 m Landsat–Sentinel-2 composite, not native 10 m Sentinel-2.
  - From memory, it has 13 classes.

**On licences (R3).** All the licences above allow publishing results. Plain CC-BY (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, TimeSen2Crop, AgriSen-COG, and the Brandenburg, Peru and IBM/NASA sets) is the safest for sharing trained models. The CC-BY-SA datasets (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet, CropHarvest) are fine for results. For released models, check how your university treats share-alike. If you want no ambiguity, start with 1, 3 or 4.

## Known, not confirmed
- **ZueriCrop:** the Hugging Face copy (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isaaccorley/zuericrop) is CC-BY-NC-4.0. I didn't check the original release or licence. A non-commercial licence may complicate sharing models.
- **Original Sen4AgriNet release (GitHub):** I didn't check its home page or licence.
- **S2Agri / PixelSet:** Hugging Face copies exist under monster-monash (CC-BY-4.0), but I didn't check the original home or access terms.
- **DENETHOR (Germany):** I searched for it but didn't confirm a record, so I can't say where it lives now.

## Not recommended, and why
- **EuroCrops:** parcel labels only, with no imagery. EuroCropsML is the version paired with Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** county-level yield prediction, not field or pixel crop type.
- **Hugging Face ZueriCrop copy:** non-commercial licence, as noted above.

## What I could not find
- Zenodo and DataCite failed intermittently during the search, so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't confirm the sizes of several entries, and I didn't get class lists for the three partial-fit datasets.
- Everything marked "from memory" above was not confirmed today.

## Next step
Download TimeSen2Crop (about 1 GB) and BreizhCrops first and get a baseline running. Then add PASTIS or EuroCropsML if you want spatial context or cross-country transfer. Before you commit to a dataset, open the dataset page or paper to confirm the class list and licence, because these were checked on 2026-11-11 and can change.

The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-W4YHCE/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** labels for specific crop types (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) per field or pixel.
- **R2 (must):** ready-made multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data already paired with those labels, so you don't build a pipeline.
- **R3 (must):** a licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- I assumed you want something you can download now, not something behind an application.

- The dataset paper compares seven deep networks and a random forest baseline, so you get ready comparisons: https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020

**4. TimeSen2Crop** (https://zenodo.org/records/4715631)
- More than 1 million pixel samples for 16 crop types, September 2017 to August 2018. Atmospherically corrected, with cloud, snow and shadow flags.
- The download is about 1 GB, and it has about 2.8k Zenodo downloads. This is the easiest one to start with.
- From memory: the area is Austria.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965

**5. AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012)
- It has Sentinel-2 cloud-optimised GeoTIFFs for five countries, labelled from farmer declarations (LPIS).
- The Zenodo record I verified holds the label part. Check the paper for where the imagery lives.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3390/rs15122980

**6. Sen4AgriNet, TACO repackaging** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco)
- It has 10,013 Sentinel-2 patches over Catalonia and France for 2019–2020, with 168 harmonised crop and land-cover classes.
- This is a third-party repackaging with a split the repackager constructed, not the original authors' release. The card says to follow the original licence, and I haven't checked that one.
- Original paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2022.3164771

**7. CropHarvest** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170)
- Global labels with time series. From memory, most labels are crop versus non-crop, with a multiclass subset. Check how many specific-crop labels you get before committing.

**8–10. Brandenburg-S12-CropVal, Peru, IBM/NASA**
- Brandenburg-S12-CropVal (https://zenodo.org/records/18632974):
  - It is a Sentinel-1/2 cropland segmentation set for cross-regional transfer.
  - I haven't read its class list, so I don't know whether the labels are specific crops.
- Peru (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051):
  - Multi-annual Sentinel-2 crop type data, new in 2026, with no papers using it yet.
  - I haven't read its class list.
- IBM/NASA (https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):
  - This is a US dataset built from HLS imagery, which is a 30 m Landsat–Sentinel-2 composite, not native 10 m Sentinel-2.
  - From memory, it has 13 classes.

**On licences (R3).** All the licences above allow publishing results. Plain CC-BY (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, TimeSen2Crop, AgriSen-COG, and the Brandenburg, Peru and IBM/NASA sets) is the safest for sharing trained models. The CC-BY-SA datasets (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet, CropHarvest) are fine for results. For released models, check how your university treats share-alike. If you want no ambiguity, start with 1, 3 or 4.

## Known, not confirmed
- **ZueriCrop:** the Hugging Face copy (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isaaccorley/zuericrop) is CC-BY-NC-4.0. I didn't check the original release or licence. A non-commercial licence may complicate sharing models.
- **Original Sen4AgriNet release (GitHub):** I didn't check its home page or licence.
- **S2Agri / PixelSet:** Hugging Face copies exist under monster-monash (CC-BY-4.0), but I didn't check the original home or access terms.
- **DENETHOR (Germany):** I searched for it but didn't confirm a record, so I can't say where it lives now.

## Not recommended, and why
- **EuroCrops:** parcel labels only, with no imagery. EuroCropsML is the version paired with Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** county-level yield prediction, not field or pixel crop type.
- **Hugging Face ZueriCrop copy:** non-commercial licence, as noted above.

## What I could not find
- Zenodo and DataCite failed intermittently during the search, so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't confirm the sizes of several entries, and I didn't get class lists for the three partial-fit datasets.
- Everything marked "from memory" above was not confirmed today.

## Details

**1. PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942)
- It has parcel-level Sentinel-2 time series patches for France, with crop-type semantic and instance labels. It's the most used benchmark of this kind, with about 93k Zenodo downloads.
- From memory, not confirmed: it has 18 crop classes, and a companion repository holds the code and splits.
- Download the 28.8 GB zip from the record page.
- It is in patch (segmentation) format rather than pixel tables, so you may need to convert it for pixel-wise or parcel-wise models.

**2. EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445)
- Per-parcel median Sentinel-2 L1C time series with 176 crop classes and 706,683 labelled points. Both figures come from the paper abstract. It was built for few-shot transfer across countries.
- It is CC-BY-SA, so derivatives must stay share-alike. Whether a trained model counts as a derivative is legally unsettled, so ask your supervisor or library.
- The data are per-parcel medians, so there is no spatial context.
- Papers that used it:
  - The dataset paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7
  - A 2026 baselines paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117

**3. BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341)
- Parcels in Brittany with both top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere Sentinel-2 series. The paper abstract says reference models, pretrained weights and a GitHub repository (dl4sits/breizhcrops) come with it.
- From memory: one region and one year (2017).
- The dataset paper compares seven deep networks and a random forest baseline, so you get ready comparisons: https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020

**4. TimeSen2Crop** (https://zenodo.org/records/4715631)

- From memory: the area is Austria.

**5. AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012)

- The Zenodo record I verified holds the label part. Check the paper for where the imagery lives.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3390/rs15122980

- This is a third-party repackaging with a split the repackager constructed, not the original authors' release. The card says to follow the original licence, and I haven't checked that one.
- Original paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/jstars.2022.3164771

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** specific crop-type labels (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) per parcel or pixel.
- **R2 (must):** Sentinel-2 time series already paired with the labels.
- **R3 (must):** a licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- I assumed you want direct downloads you can start on today, with no application process.

## Shortlist
Everything below is a direct download with no account. Registry records for access and licence were checked on 2026-11-11. Class lists and region details are from my memory of the papers unless noted.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PASTIS-R](https://zenodo.org/records/5735646) (also S2-only PASTIS, France) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 54 GB | Strong | Registry today; contents from memory |
| 2 | [BreizhCrops](https://zenodo.org/records/14585341) (Brittany, France) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 7.7 GB | Strong | Registry and paper abstract today |
| 3 | [S2-Agri Pixel-Set](https://zenodo.org/records/5815488) and [Patch](https://zenodo.org/records/5815523) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 11 GB each | Strong | Registry today |
| 4 | [EuroCropsML](https://zenodo.org/records/15095445) (Estonia, Latvia, Portugal, 2021) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 4.8 GB | Strong | Registry and paper today |
| 5 | [AgriSen-COG](https://zenodo.org/records/7892012) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 28 GB | Strong (see caveat) | Registry today |
| 6 | [PixelSet-Slovakia](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21885996) (2020–2025) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 31 GB | Partial | Registry today |
| 7 | [CropHarvest](https://zenodo.org/records/10251170) (global) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 28 GB | Partial | Registry today |
| 8 | [Sen4AgriNet (TACO copy)](https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 10K–100K samples | Partial | Registry today |

## Details
- **PASTIS-R** is the standard benchmark, with the most published baselines to compare against.
  - It has parcel-level labels for about 18 crop classes (from memory) on S2 time-series patches, plus Sentinel-1.
  - Cite: [Garnot & Landrieu, ICCV 2021](https://doi.org/10.1109/iccv48922.2021.00483).
- **BreizhCrops** has one time series per parcel, so it suits sequence models (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN) and a master's timeline.
  - It has both top-of-atmosphere and surface-reflectance S2 data, according to the paper abstract.
  - It covers a single region and year, so expect weak generalisation claims.
  - Cite: [Rußwurm et al., 2020](https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020).
- **S2-Agri** is parcel-based, from one S2 tile in France. It's the dataset behind the Pixel-Set Encoder and Temporal Attention Encoder papers.
- **EuroCropsML** is built for few-shot and transfer experiments across three countries.
  - It uses the EuroCrops harmonised crop taxonomy.
  - Cite: [Reuss et al., Scientific Data 2025](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7).
  - **Licence:** CC-BY-SA requires derived *datasets* to stay share-alike. Whether that extends to trained models is unsettled, so ask your supervisor or library before you release weights.
- **AgriSen-COG** is large and covers several countries.
  - The registry text confirms rasterised parcel labels. I did not confirm how the S2 imagery is packaged, so read the linked paper first.
- **PixelSet-Slovakia** is new, with 75k parcel-years across six years, so it's good for cross-year domain adaptation. It has little published baseline work.
- **CropHarvest** is mostly crop vs non-crop. Only a subset has multi-class labels, and the satellite data is pre-extracted features rather than raw pixels.
- **Sen4AgriNet** is a third-party repackaging. Check the original release's terms before relying on the licence shown here.

## Known, not confirmed
- **ZueriCrop** (Switzerland) fits well, but the only copy I found declares CC-BY-NC-4.0. That's a redistribution, so check the original authors' repository before sharing models.
- **Datasets I recalled but could not find in the registries:** DENETHOR (Germany), TimeMatch, Munich480/MTLCC, SICKLE, and the African crop-type sets formerly on Radiant MLHub. Check their project pages before using them. I can't vouch for their access or licence.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[IBM-NASA multi-temporal crop classification](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):** open, CC-BY-4.0 and well labelled (US CDL), but the imagery is HLS (Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2), not pure Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** built for yield prediction, not crop-type labels.
- **German national crop-type maps (Zenodo):** these are output maps rather than training data paired with S2, and the registry lists them as restricted.

## What I could not find
- Zenodo, DataCite and HuggingFace returned some invalid responses during the searches, so the list may be incomplete.
- I did not read the dataset documentation pages to check class lists and sample counts. Confirm those in each paper.

## Next step
Start with **BreizhCrops** for a quick baseline, then use **PASTIS-R** or **EuroCropsML** for the main results. Before you pick a share-alike dataset, find out what your university requires for releasing model weights.

The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-OVwKax/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

- **R1 (must):** specific crop-type labels (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) per parcel or pixel.
- **R2 (must):** Sentinel-2 time series already paired with the labels.
- **R3 (must):** a licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- I assumed you want direct downloads you can start on today, with no application process.

## Shortlist
Everything below is a direct download with no account. Registry records for access and licence were checked on 2026-11-11. Class lists and region details are from my memory of the papers unless noted.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PASTIS-R](https://zenodo.org/records/5735646) (also S2-only PASTIS, France) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 54 GB | Strong | Registry today; contents from memory |
| 2 | [BreizhCrops](https://zenodo.org/records/14585341) (Brittany, France) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 7.7 GB | Strong | Registry and paper abstract today |
| 3 | [S2-Agri Pixel-Set](https://zenodo.org/records/5815488) and [Patch](https://zenodo.org/records/5815523) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 11 GB each | Strong | Registry today |
| 4 | [EuroCropsML](https://zenodo.org/records/15095445) (Estonia, Latvia, Portugal, 2021) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 4.8 GB | Strong | Registry and paper today |
| 5 | [AgriSen-COG](https://zenodo.org/records/7892012) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 28 GB | Strong (see caveat) | Registry today |
| 6 | [PixelSet-Slovakia](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21885996) (2020–2025) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 31 GB | Partial | Registry today |
| 7 | [CropHarvest](https://zenodo.org/records/10251170) (global) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 28 GB | Partial | Registry today |
| 8 | [Sen4AgriNet (TACO copy)](https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 10K–100K samples | Partial | Registry today |

## Details
- **PASTIS-R** is the standard benchmark, with the most published baselines to compare against.
  - It has parcel-level labels for about 18 crop classes (from memory) on S2 time-series patches, plus Sentinel-1.
  - Cite: [Garnot & Landrieu, ICCV 2021](https://doi.org/10.1109/iccv48922.2021.00483).
- **BreizhCrops** has one time series per parcel, so it suits sequence models (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN) and a master's timeline.
  - It has both top-of-atmosphere and surface-reflectance S2 data, according to the paper abstract.
  - It covers a single region and year, so expect weak generalisation claims.
  - Cite: [Rußwurm et al., 2020](https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020).
- **S2-Agri** is parcel-based, from one S2 tile in France. It's the dataset behind the Pixel-Set Encoder and Temporal Attention Encoder papers.
- **EuroCropsML** is built for few-shot and transfer experiments across three countries.
  - It uses the EuroCrops harmonised crop taxonomy.
  - Cite: [Reuss et al., Scientific Data 2025](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7).
  - **Licence:** CC-BY-SA requires derived *datasets* to stay share-alike. Whether that extends to trained models is unsettled, so ask your supervisor or library before you release weights.
- **AgriSen-COG** is large and covers several countries.
  - The registry text confirms rasterised parcel labels. I did not confirm how the S2 imagery is packaged, so read the linked paper first.
- **PixelSet-Slovakia** is new, with 75k parcel-years across six years, so it's good for cross-year domain adaptation. It has little published baseline work.
- **CropHarvest** is mostly crop vs non-crop. Only a subset has multi-class labels, and the satellite data is pre-extracted features rather than raw pixels.
- **Sen4AgriNet** is a third-party repackaging. Check the original release's terms before relying on the licence shown here.

## Known, not confirmed
- **ZueriCrop** (Switzerland) fits well, but the only copy I found declares CC-BY-NC-4.0. That's a redistribution, so check the original authors' repository before sharing models.
- **Datasets I recalled but could not find in the registries:** DENETHOR (Germany), TimeMatch, Munich480/MTLCC, SICKLE, and the African crop-type sets formerly on Radiant MLHub. Check their project pages before using them. I can't vouch for their access or licence.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[IBM-NASA multi-temporal crop classification](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):** open, CC-BY-4.0 and well labelled (US CDL), but the imagery is HLS (Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2), not pure Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** built for yield prediction, not crop-type labels.
- **German national crop-type maps (Zenodo):** these are output maps rather than training data paired with S2, and the registry lists them as restricted.

## What I could not find
- Zenodo, DataCite and HuggingFace returned some invalid responses during the searches, so the list may be incomplete.
- I did not read the dataset documentation pages to check class lists and sample counts. Confirm those in each paper.

## Next step
Start with **BreizhCrops** for a quick baseline, then use **PASTIS-R** or **EuroCropsML** for the main results. Before you pick a share-alike dataset, find out what your university requires for releasing model weights.

The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-OVwKax/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

- **Datasets I recalled but could not find in the registries:** DENETHOR (Germany), TimeMatch, Munich480/MTLCC, SICKLE, and the African crop-type sets formerly on Radiant MLHub. Check their project pages before using them. I can't vouch for their access or licence.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[IBM-NASA multi-temporal crop classification](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):** open, CC-BY-4.0 and well labelled (US CDL), but the imagery is HLS (Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2), not pure Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** built for yield prediction, not crop-type labels.
- **German national crop-type maps (Zenodo):** these are output maps rather than training data paired with S2, and the registry lists them as restricted.

## What I could not find
- Zenodo, DataCite and HuggingFace returned some invalid responses during the searches, so the list may be incomplete.
- I did not read the dataset documentation pages to check class lists and sample counts. Confirm those in each paper.

## Next step
Start with **BreizhCrops** for a quick baseline, then use **PASTIS-R** or **EuroCropsML** for the main results. Before you pick a share-alike dataset, find out what your university requires for releasing model weights.

## Shortlist
Everything below is a direct download with no account. Registry records for access and licence were checked on 2026-11-11. Class lists and region details are from my memory of the papers unless noted.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PASTIS-R](https://zenodo.org/records/5735646) (also S2-only PASTIS, France) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 54 GB | Strong | Registry today; contents from memory |
| 2 | [BreizhCrops](https://zenodo.org/records/14585341) (Brittany, France) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 7.7 GB | Strong | Registry and paper abstract today |
| 3 | [S2-Agri Pixel-Set](https://zenodo.org/records/5815488) and [Patch](https://zenodo.org/records/5815523) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 11 GB each | Strong | Registry today |
| 4 | [EuroCropsML](https://zenodo.org/records/15095445) (Estonia, Latvia, Portugal, 2021) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 4.8 GB | Strong | Registry and paper today |
| 5 | [AgriSen-COG](https://zenodo.org/records/7892012) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 28 GB | Strong (see caveat) | Registry today |
| 6 | [PixelSet-Slovakia](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21885996) (2020–2025) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 31 GB | Partial | Registry today |
| 7 | [CropHarvest](https://zenodo.org/records/10251170) (global) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 28 GB | Partial | Registry today |
| 8 | [Sen4AgriNet (TACO copy)](https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco) | Open download | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 10K–100K samples | Partial | Registry today |

## Details
- **PASTIS-R** is the standard benchmark, with the most published baselines to compare against.
  - It has parcel-level labels for about 18 crop classes (from memory) on S2 time-series patches, plus Sentinel-1.
  - Cite: [Garnot & Landrieu, ICCV 2021](https://doi.org/10.1109/iccv48922.2021.00483).
- **BreizhCrops** has one time series per parcel, so it suits sequence models (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN) and a master's timeline.
  - It has both top-of-atmosphere and surface-reflectance S2 data, according to the paper abstract.
  - It covers a single region and year, so expect weak generalisation claims.
  - Cite: [Rußwurm et al., 2020](https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020).
- **S2-Agri** is parcel-based, from one S2 tile in France. It's the dataset behind the Pixel-Set Encoder and Temporal Attention Encoder papers.
- **EuroCropsML** is built for few-shot and transfer experiments across three countries.
  - It uses the EuroCrops harmonised crop taxonomy.
  - Cite: [Reuss et al., Scientific Data 2025](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7).
  - **Licence:** CC-BY-SA requires derived *datasets* to stay share-alike. Whether that extends to trained models is unsettled, so ask your supervisor or library before you release weights.
- **AgriSen-COG** is large and covers several countries.
  - The registry text confirms rasterised parcel labels. I did not confirm how the S2 imagery is packaged, so read the linked paper first.
- **PixelSet-Slovakia** is new, with 75k parcel-years across six years, so it's good for cross-year domain adaptation. It has little published baseline work.
- **CropHarvest** is mostly crop vs non-crop. Only a subset has multi-class labels, and the satellite data is pre-extracted features rather than raw pixels.
- **Sen4AgriNet** is a third-party repackaging. Check the original release's terms before relying on the licence shown here.

## Known, not confirmed
- **ZueriCrop** (Switzerland) fits well, but the only copy I found declares CC-BY-NC-4.0. That's a redistribution, so check the original authors' repository before sharing models.
- **Datasets I recalled but could not find in the registries:** DENETHOR (Germany), TimeMatch, Munich480/MTLCC, SICKLE, and the African crop-type sets formerly on Radiant MLHub. Check their project pages before using them. I can't vouch for their access or licence.

## Not recommended, and why
- **[IBM-NASA multi-temporal crop classification](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-nasa-geospatial/multi-temporal-crop-classification):** open, CC-BY-4.0 and well labelled (US CDL), but the imagery is HLS (Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2), not pure Sentinel-2.
- **CropNet:** built for yield prediction, not crop-type labels.
- **German national crop-type maps (Zenodo):** these are output maps rather than training data paired with S2, and the registry lists them as restricted.

## What I could not find

- I did not read the dataset documentation pages to check class lists and sample counts. Confirm those in each paper.

✓ pass 01-recall-breizhcrops

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
These are the benchmarks I'd shortlist. I'm working from memory and haven't checked the licence pages live, so confirm each licence on the dataset's own page before you publish.

## Best fits (field or parcel-level, Sentinel-2 time series included)

| Dataset | Region / labels | What you get | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 18 crop classes plus background, ~2,400 parcel-annotated patches | Sentinel-2 time series (PASTIS-R adds Sentinel-1). Parcel-level semantic and panoptic labels. A standard benchmark, so you can compare against published results. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo ("PASTIS"), code at `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark` on GitHub |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, ~600k field-level time series, 9 main crop classes | Sentinel-2 L1C and L2A pixel-aggregated per field. It has a PyTorch loader and is easy to use for per-field sequence models (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN). | CC-BY 4.0 (code MIT) | `dl4sits/BreizhCrops` on GitHub, Zenodo |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, multi-year, hierarchical labels (many classes) | Sentinel-2 time series with parcel labels from CAP declarations. It is large, so a good fit if you want scale. | CC-BY 4.0 | GitHub (`Orion-AI-Lab/S4A`) |
| **CropHarvest** | Global, point-level labels, a multi-class subset for crop types | Pre-extracted Sentinel-2, Sentinel-1, ERA5 and SRTM time series per point. It has good coverage outside Europe, but labels are sparse and uneven by region. | Mostly CC-BY-SA 4.0 (varies by sub-dataset) | Zenodo, `nasaharvest/cropharvest` on GitHub |
| **ZueriCrop** | Zurich and Thurgau, Switzerland, ~48 classes | Sentinel-2 time series with instance-level field labels. | Check the repo | `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` on GitHub |
| **Radiant MLHub / Source Cooperative crop-type sets** | South Africa (Western Cape), Kenya, Ghana, Tanzania and others | Smallholder-region field labels paired with Sentinel-2 (and sometimes Sentinel-1). Radiant MLHub was migrated to Source Cooperative in 2024, so look there. | Mostly CC-BY 4.0, per dataset | source.coop |

## Also worth knowing
- **EuroCrops** has harmonised EU LPIS crop labels with a common taxonomy (HCAT). It is labels only, with no Sentinel-2 attached, so you'd have to build the pairing yourself. Skip it given your time constraint, unless you need many countries. The licence is CC-BY-SA 4.0.
- **Munich480** (Bavaria) and **TimeMatch** (several regions, aimed at domain adaptation) are Sentinel-2 crop-type sets. Check their licences before relying on them.
- **GEO-Bench and PANGAEA** bundle several crop-type tasks, including PASTIS-R and a South Africa crop-type set. They give you ready-made splits and baselines, which helps if you want a comparison table.

## Recommendation
- **Start with PASTIS-R.** It is parcel-level, covers many crop types and has permissive licensing. It has the most published baselines, so your results will be comparable.
- **Add BreizhCrops** if you want a lightweight per-field time-series task, or if you want a second dataset to show your model generalises.
- **Add CropHarvest or a Source Cooperative set** if you want a non-European test region.

## Licence points to check
- **CC-BY** (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, Sen4AgriNet) only needs attribution. It allows publishing results and sharing trained models.
- **CC-BY-SA** (CropHarvest, EuroCrops) is also fine for publishing results. The share-alike clause applies clearly to redistributed or derived datasets. Whether it reaches trained model weights is legally unsettled, so read the terms or ask your supervisor if you plan to release weights.
- Many of these labels come from government LPIS or CAP data under open-government licences, and the Sentinel-2 imagery is free and open under Copernicus terms. Cite the original data providers as well as the benchmark paper.

I can fetch the current licence text and download links for any of these if you want. I'd need to use web access for that.

| Dataset | Region / labels | What you get | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 18 crop classes plus background, ~2,400 parcel-annotated patches | Sentinel-2 time series (PASTIS-R adds Sentinel-1). Parcel-level semantic and panoptic labels. A standard benchmark, so you can compare against published results. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo ("PASTIS"), code at `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark` on GitHub |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, ~600k field-level time series, 9 main crop classes | Sentinel-2 L1C and L2A pixel-aggregated per field. It has a PyTorch loader and is easy to use for per-field sequence models (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN). | CC-BY 4.0 (code MIT) | `dl4sits/BreizhCrops` on GitHub, Zenodo |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, multi-year, hierarchical labels (many classes) | Sentinel-2 time series with parcel labels from CAP declarations. It is large, so a good fit if you want scale. | CC-BY 4.0 | GitHub (`Orion-AI-Lab/S4A`) |
| **CropHarvest** | Global, point-level labels, a multi-class subset for crop types | Pre-extracted Sentinel-2, Sentinel-1, ERA5 and SRTM time series per point. It has good coverage outside Europe, but labels are sparse and uneven by region. | Mostly CC-BY-SA 4.0 (varies by sub-dataset) | Zenodo, `nasaharvest/cropharvest` on GitHub |
| **ZueriCrop** | Zurich and Thurgau, Switzerland, ~48 classes | Sentinel-2 time series with instance-level field labels. | Check the repo | `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` on GitHub |
| **Radiant MLHub / Source Cooperative crop-type sets** | South Africa (Western Cape), Kenya, Ghana, Tanzania and others | Smallholder-region field labels paired with Sentinel-2 (and sometimes Sentinel-1). Radiant MLHub was migrated to Source Cooperative in 2024, so look there. | Mostly CC-BY 4.0, per dataset | source.coop |

## Also worth knowing
- **EuroCrops** has harmonised EU LPIS crop labels with a common taxonomy (HCAT). It is labels only, with no Sentinel-2 attached, so you'd have to build the pairing yourself. Skip it given your time constraint, unless you need many countries. The licence is CC-BY-SA 4.0.
- **Munich480** (Bavaria) and **TimeMatch** (several regions, aimed at domain adaptation) are Sentinel-2 crop-type sets. Check their licences before relying on them.
- **GEO-Bench and PANGAEA** bundle several crop-type tasks, including PASTIS-R and a South Africa crop-type set. They give you ready-made splits and baselines, which helps if you want a comparison table.

## Recommendation
- **Start with PASTIS-R.** It is parcel-level, covers many crop types and has permissive licensing. It has the most published baselines, so your results will be comparable.
- **Add BreizhCrops** if you want a lightweight per-field time-series task, or if you want a second dataset to show your model generalises.
- **Add CropHarvest or a Source Cooperative set** if you want a non-European test region.

## Licence points to check
- **CC-BY** (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, Sen4AgriNet) only needs attribution. It allows publishing results and sharing trained models.
- **CC-BY-SA** (CropHarvest, EuroCrops) is also fine for publishing results. The share-alike clause applies clearly to redistributed or derived datasets. Whether it reaches trained model weights is legally unsettled, so read the terms or ask your supervisor if you plan to release weights.
- Many of these labels come from government LPIS or CAP data under open-government licences, and the Sentinel-2 imagery is free and open under Copernicus terms. Cite the original data providers as well as the benchmark paper.

I can fetch the current licence text and download links for any of these if you want. I'd need to use web access for that.

I haven't checked these against the live download pages, so the details below come from memory. Licences are the part most likely to be wrong, so check each Zenodo or GitHub record before you commit to a dataset. Sentinel-2 itself is open under Copernicus terms, so the licence that matters is the one on the labels and the packaged product.

## Best fits (S2 time series already paired with crop-type labels)

| Dataset | Labels and coverage | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px. Parcel-level labels for 18 crop classes. Full-year S2 series. PASTIS-R adds Sentinel-1. | CC BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "PASTIS-R"). Code is in the GitHub repo `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark`. |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, 2017. About 600k parcels, with S2 L1C and L2A series and about 9 crop classes. It's the standard pixel- or parcel-level time-series benchmark. | Open data based on the French RPG (Licence Ouverte/Etalab), code under MIT. Check the record. | GitHub `dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, with a `pip install breizhcrops` loader. |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal. About 700k parcels with S2 time series, built from EuroCrops labels. It's designed for transfer and few-shot learning. | CC BY 4.0 | Zenodo, with a `eurocropsml` Python package. |
| **S2-Agri** | Île-de-France, 2018. About 190k parcels with S2 pixel sets and 20 classes. | Verify on Zenodo | Linked from `VSainteuf/lightweight-temporal-attention-pytorch`. |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020. Very large, with parcel-level labels derived from CAP declarations (EU farm subsidy records). | I believe CC BY 4.0, so verify. | GitHub `Orion-AI-Lab/S4A` |
| **ZueriCrop** | Switzerland, 2019. About 116k fields with a hierarchical label set of up to 48 classes. | Unclear, so check before relying on it. | GitHub `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` |
| **CropHarvest** | Global point labels with multi-class crop types, plus preprocessed S2, S1 and ERA5 series. Multi-class labels cover a much smaller subset than the binary crop/non-crop labels. | Mostly CC BY-SA 4.0, mixed by sub-dataset | GitHub `nasaharvest/cropharvest`, data on Zenodo |

Outside Europe, look at the Kenya, Ghana, South Sudan and Western Cape (South Africa) crop-type sets that were on Radiant MLHub. They moved to Source Cooperative, and most were CC BY 4.0.

## What I'd pick
- **For segmentation or panoptic parcel work** (U-TAE, Transformers), use **PASTIS-R**. It has the cleanest licence and the most citations, so your results are easy to compare.
- **For pixel or parcel time-series classification** (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN), use **BreizhCrops**. **EuroCropsML** is a good second dataset for cross-region generalisation.
- **For an added domain-shift or generalisation experiment**, use **CropHarvest** or one of the African sets.

## Licence caveats
- **CC BY** is fine for publishing results and sharing models.
- **CC BY-SA** (CropHarvest) is probably fine for sharing models, but it can oblige you to share derived datasets under the same licence. Check how it applies to your case.
- **EuroCrops** (the harmonised EU parcel labels) is labels only, with no imagery, and its per-country licences vary. Use EuroCropsML instead if you want imagery and labels together.

I can look up the actual licence text on each Zenodo record to confirm the ones I flagged.

Outside Europe, look at the Kenya, Ghana, South Sudan and Western Cape (South Africa) crop-type sets that were on Radiant MLHub. They moved to Source Cooperative, and most were CC BY 4.0.

## What I'd pick
- **For segmentation or panoptic parcel work** (U-TAE, Transformers), use **PASTIS-R**. It has the cleanest licence and the most citations, so your results are easy to compare.
- **For pixel or parcel time-series classification** (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN), use **BreizhCrops**. **EuroCropsML** is a good second dataset for cross-region generalisation.
- **For an added domain-shift or generalisation experiment**, use **CropHarvest** or one of the African sets.

## Licence caveats
- **CC BY** is fine for publishing results and sharing models.
- **CC BY-SA** (CropHarvest) is probably fine for sharing models, but it can oblige you to share derived datasets under the same licence. Check how it applies to your case.
- **EuroCrops** (the harmonised EU parcel labels) is labels only, with no imagery, and its per-country licences vary. Use EuroCropsML instead if you want imagery and labels together.

I can look up the actual licence text on each Zenodo record to confirm the ones I flagged.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I haven't checked these against the live download pages, so the details below come from memory. Licences are the part most likely to be wrong, so check each Zenodo or GitHub record before you commit to a dataset. Sentinel-2 itself is open under Copernicus terms, so the licence that matters is the one on the labels and the packaged product.

## Best fits (S2 time series already paired with crop-type labels)

| Dataset | Labels and coverage | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px. Parcel-level labels for 18 crop classes. Full-year S2 series. PASTIS-R adds Sentinel-1. | CC BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "PASTIS-R"). Code is in the GitHub repo `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark`. |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, 2017. About 600k parcels, with S2 L1C and L2A series and about 9 crop classes. It's the standard pixel- or parcel-level time-series benchmark. | Open data based on the French RPG (Licence Ouverte/Etalab), code under MIT. Check the record. | GitHub `dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, with a `pip install breizhcrops` loader. |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal. About 700k parcels with S2 time series, built from EuroCrops labels. It's designed for transfer and few-shot learning. | CC BY 4.0 | Zenodo, with a `eurocropsml` Python package. |
| **S2-Agri** | Île-de-France, 2018. About 190k parcels with S2 pixel sets and 20 classes. | Verify on Zenodo | Linked from `VSainteuf/lightweight-temporal-attention-pytorch`. |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020. Very large, with parcel-level labels derived from CAP declarations (EU farm subsidy records). | I believe CC BY 4.0, so verify. | GitHub `Orion-AI-Lab/S4A` |
| **ZueriCrop** | Switzerland, 2019. About 116k fields with a hierarchical label set of up to 48 classes. | Unclear, so check before relying on it. | GitHub `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` |
| **CropHarvest** | Global point labels with multi-class crop types, plus preprocessed S2, S1 and ERA5 series. Multi-class labels cover a much smaller subset than the binary crop/non-crop labels. | Mostly CC BY-SA 4.0, mixed by sub-dataset | GitHub `nasaharvest/cropharvest`, data on Zenodo |

Outside Europe, look at the Kenya, Ghana, South Sudan and Western Cape (South Africa) crop-type sets that were on Radiant MLHub. They moved to Source Cooperative, and most were CC BY 4.0.

## What I'd pick
- **For segmentation or panoptic parcel work** (U-TAE, Transformers), use **PASTIS-R**. It has the cleanest licence and the most citations, so your results are easy to compare.
- **For pixel or parcel time-series classification** (LSTM, Transformer, TempCNN), use **BreizhCrops**. **EuroCropsML** is a good second dataset for cross-region generalisation.
- **For an added domain-shift or generalisation experiment**, use **CropHarvest** or one of the African sets.

## Licence caveats
- **CC BY** is fine for publishing results and sharing models.
- **CC BY-SA** (CropHarvest) is probably fine for sharing models, but it can oblige you to share derived datasets under the same licence. Check how it applies to your case.
- **EuroCrops** (the harmonised EU parcel labels) is labels only, with no imagery, and its per-country licences vary. Use EuroCropsML instead if you want imagery and labels together.

Prompt

I'm doing a master's project on crop type classification from Sentinel-2 image time series. I need ground-truth labels for actual crops (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on, not just cropland versus non-cropland) for individual fields or pixels, already paired with multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data, because I don't have time to build my own download and preprocessing pipeline. The data also needs a licence that lets me publish results and share trained models. Which benchmark datasets fit, and where do I get them?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-breizhcropsllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the BreizhCrops dataset (also written Breizhcrops: Sentinel-2 time series of agricultural parcels in Brittany, France, with crop type labels, by Russwurm and colleagues) as a candidate crop type classification benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-pastisllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the PASTIS dataset (Panoptic Agricultural Satellite TIme Series, by Sainte Fare Garnot and Landrieu; any variant such as PASTIS-R) as a candidate crop type benchmark built on Sentinel-2 time series. FAIL if the answer does not mention PASTIS, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-sen4agrinetllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Sen4AgriNet dataset (also known as S4A, the Sentinel-2 multi-year, multi-country crop classification benchmark covering Catalonia and France, by Sykas and colleagues) as a candidate crop type benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-timesen2cropllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the TimeSen2Crop dataset (about one million labelled Sentinel-2 time-series samples for 16 crop types in Austria, by Weikmann, Paris and Bruzzone) as a candidate crop type benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-pastis-zenodo-cc-byllm

PASS if the final answer states that PASTIS (Panoptic Agricultural Satellite TIme Series) can be downloaded openly, without an application, and that its licence is Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0). Saying that it is hosted on Zenodo is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention PASTIS, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial or share-alike one), or says access must be requested.

06-access-sen4agrinet-mitllm

PASS if the final answer states that Sen4AgriNet (S4A) can be downloaded openly, without an application (for example from the Hugging Face Hub, Dropbox or Google Drive), and that it is released under the MIT licence. FAIL if the answer does not mention Sen4AgriNet, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence, or says access must be requested.

07-access-timesen2crop-zenodo-cc-byllm

PASS if the final answer states that TimeSen2Crop can be downloaded openly, without an application, and that its licence is Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0). Saying that it is hosted on Zenodo is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention TimeSen2Crop, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence, or says access must be requested.

08-trap-eurosat-no-crop-typesllm

PASS if the final answer does not present EuroSAT (the Sentinel-2 land use and land cover patch dataset of 27,000 images in 10 classes, by Helber and colleagues) as providing crop type labels or multi-temporal Sentinel-2 time series. Not mentioning EuroSAT passes. Mentioning it while saying that it has only single-date patches and only broad classes (annual crop, permanent crop, pasture) rather than crop species passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists EuroSAT as a crop type classification dataset for this project without stating that limitation.

09-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known crop type datasets that pair labels with Sentinel-2 time series: EuroCropsML (the ready-to-use machine learning version of EuroCrops for Latvia, Portugal and Estonia, by Reuss and colleagues); or the South Africa Crop Type Competition dataset (also called the Western Cape crop type dataset or the Radiant Earth "Spot the Crop" challenge data, on Radiant MLHub or Source Cooperative). FAIL if the answer mentions neither of them.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Yes, you can start today. The best-fitting datasets (GoiEner, London, Norwegian iFlex, Swiss HEAPO) are open downloads. The Irish CER trial needs a signed form, and the large UK SERL data needs months of accreditation.

I checked these on 2026-11-12 against Zenodo, the London Datastore, data.gov.au and the Pecan Street site. Three official pages (ISSDA, SERL, Ausgrid's own) blocked or failed my tool, so those access details come from web-search summaries and memory.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** per-household smart-meter series, hundreds of households or more.
- **R2 (must):** at least a year of history per household.
- **R3 (must):** hourly or finer resolution.
- I assumed non-commercial research use, and that you'd rather start now than wait months.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [GoiEner](https://zenodo.org/records/7362094) (Spain) | Open download, minutes | CC BY (inconsistent, see below) | 25,559 raw series, hourly, 2014–2022 | Strong | Today |
| 2 | [Low Carbon London](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d) | Open download, minutes | Not stated in what I read | 5,567 households, half-hourly, 2011–14 | Strong | Today |
| 3 | [iFlex](https://zenodo.org/records/8248802) (Norway) | Open download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | Hourly, 2020–21 | Partial | Today |
| 4 | [HEAPO](https://zenodo.org/records/15056919) (Switzerland) | Open download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 1,408 homes, 15-min, 2018–24 | Partial | Today |
| 5 | [Irish CER trial](https://www.ucd.ie/issda/data/commissionforenergyregulationcer/) | Signed request form, days | ISSDA terms | >5,000 homes and businesses, half-hourly | Strong | Web search and memory |
| 6 | [SERL Observatory](https://datacatalogue.ukdataservice.ac.uk/studies/study/8666) (UK) | Accredited-researcher application, months | UKDS secure agreement | ~13k homes, half-hourly, 2019–24 | Strong | Web search |
| 7 | [Smart-Grid Smart-City](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/smart-grid-smart-city-customer-trial-data) (Australia) | Open download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | Half-hourly, 2010–14 | Partial | Today |
| 8 | [Ausgrid Solar Home](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/nsw-solar-home-electricty-data) | Open, but see caveat | Not checked | 300 homes, half-hourly, 3 years | Partial | Today |
| 9 | [Pecan Street Dataport](https://www.pecanstreet.org/dataport/) (US) | Free academic account, days | Non-commercial only | 75 homes in the free tier, 1-min | Partial | Today |

## Details
**GoiEner** (start here for scale)
- Hourly kWh for a Spanish energy cooperative's customers, 2014-11 to 2022-06.
- It comes as raw (2 GB) and imputed files (about 0.5–0.8 GB each), plus a metadata file. The imputed files only include series longer than a year, which covers your R2.
- It includes some small businesses, farms and industrial tariffs. Filter on the tariff column (2.X is households and small businesses).
- The Zenodo page says CC-BY-SA in the description but CC BY 4.0 in the rights field. Cite the WHY project.
- The 2020–21 pandemic period is in the data.

**Low Carbon London**
- This is the most-used UK benchmark: a representative sample, about 167 million rows and 10 GB unzipped.
- About 1,100 households were on dynamic time-of-use prices in 2013, with the price given a day ahead. Treat them as a confound for day-ahead forecasting, or exclude them.
- Many households have less than the full 2.3 years.
- A published day-ahead benchmark used 71 filtered households from it: [Benchmarking of Load Forecasting Methods Using Residential Smart Meter Data](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12199844) (2022).

**Irish CER trial**
- Download the Research Purposes request form from the ISSDA page, sign it and email it to issda@ucd.ie.
- It runs about 1.5 years (from memory) and mixes homes and businesses, so you'll need to filter to residential.
- Don't use unofficial mirrors. A ScienceDB copy turned up in the registry, and I didn't verify its permissions.

**SERL**
- You need Accredited Researcher status through the ONS accreditation service (every team member), the UKDS Secure Access agreement and approval from the SERL governance board.
- You can't download the data, and you must be in the UK when you access it.
- SERL's site quotes 4–6 weeks for review. The UKDS guidance says 3–4 months overall.
- There is also a [safeguarded SERL statistical dataset](https://doi.org/10.5255/ukda-sn-8963-1), but it is aggregated, so it doesn't meet your per-household requirement.

**Pecan Street**
- The free tier covers 75 homes (25 each in Austin, New York and California) for non-commercial academic use.
- Paid university tiers start at $10,000. That is too small for "hundreds of households" unless you pay.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** the page confirms half-hourly data for the trial, but I haven't checked per-customer history. I recall about 13,000 customers, and many have partial coverage.
- **Ausgrid Solar Home:** all 300 homes have rooftop PV, so consumption and generation are separate channels. One user's quality screening kept only about 54 homes. Ausgrid's own page returned 404 to my tool. Check whether the data.gov.au link still serves the files.
- **iFlex:** it spans about two winters (early 2020 to spring 2021), so many households may have under a year. Check the lengths.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Electricity Load Diagrams (UCI, 370 clients):** Portuguese clients that are mostly commercial or industrial, not homes. It is a common forecasting benchmark, so it's easy to mistake for household data.
- **UK-DALE, REFIT, ECO:** these are household datasets, but they have only about 5 to 20 homes each (from memory).
- **Synthetic sets (ResStock-based, LPG-derived, OpenSynth):** simulated rather than measured.

## What I could not find
- I did not check per-household history length inside any dataset. For R2, count households with at least 365 days after you download.
- I found no other open household set at this scale with 1+ years of hourly data. The registry search also returned many small or country-specific sets that I did not review individually.

## Next step
Download GoiEner (filtered to household tariffs) and Low Carbon London (excluding the time-of-use group) now, and send the ISSDA form in parallel since it takes days. Only start the SERL accreditation if you need UK data from after 2014, because it takes months.

- Paid university tiers start at $10,000. That is too small for "hundreds of households" unless you pay.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** the page confirms half-hourly data for the trial, but I haven't checked per-customer history. I recall about 13,000 customers, and many have partial coverage.
- **Ausgrid Solar Home:** all 300 homes have rooftop PV, so consumption and generation are separate channels. One user's quality screening kept only about 54 homes. Ausgrid's own page returned 404 to my tool. Check whether the data.gov.au link still serves the files.
- **iFlex:** it spans about two winters (early 2020 to spring 2021), so many households may have under a year. Check the lengths.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Electricity Load Diagrams (UCI, 370 clients):** Portuguese clients that are mostly commercial or industrial, not homes. It is a common forecasting benchmark, so it's easy to mistake for household data.
- **UK-DALE, REFIT, ECO:** these are household datasets, but they have only about 5 to 20 homes each (from memory).
- **Synthetic sets (ResStock-based, LPG-derived, OpenSynth):** simulated rather than measured.

## What I could not find
- I did not check per-household history length inside any dataset. For R2, count households with at least 365 days after you download.
- I found no other open household set at this scale with 1+ years of hourly data. The registry search also returned many small or country-specific sets that I did not review individually.

## Next step
Download GoiEner (filtered to household tariffs) and Low Carbon London (excluding the time-of-use group) now, and send the ISSDA form in parallel since it takes days. Only start the SERL accreditation if you need UK data from after 2014, because it takes months.

✓ pass 04-recall-pecan-street

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Yes, you can start today. The best-fitting datasets (GoiEner, London, Norwegian iFlex, Swiss HEAPO) are open downloads. The Irish CER trial needs a signed form, and the large UK SERL data needs months of accreditation.

I checked these on 2026-11-12 against Zenodo, the London Datastore, data.gov.au and the Pecan Street site. Three official pages (ISSDA, SERL, Ausgrid's own) blocked or failed my tool, so those access details come from web-search summaries and memory.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** per-household smart-meter series, hundreds of households or more.
- **R2 (must):** at least a year of history per household.
- **R3 (must):** hourly or finer resolution.
- I assumed non-commercial research use, and that you'd rather start now than wait months.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [GoiEner](https://zenodo.org/records/7362094) (Spain) | Open download, minutes | CC BY (inconsistent, see below) | 25,559 raw series, hourly, 2014–2022 | Strong | Today |
| 2 | [Low Carbon London](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d) | Open download, minutes | Not stated in what I read | 5,567 households, half-hourly, 2011–14 | Strong | Today |
| 3 | [iFlex](https://zenodo.org/records/8248802) (Norway) | Open download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | Hourly, 2020–21 | Partial | Today |
| 4 | [HEAPO](https://zenodo.org/records/15056919) (Switzerland) | Open download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 1,408 homes, 15-min, 2018–24 | Partial | Today |
| 5 | [Irish CER trial](https://www.ucd.ie/issda/data/commissionforenergyregulationcer/) | Signed request form, days | ISSDA terms | >5,000 homes and businesses, half-hourly | Strong | Web search and memory |
| 6 | [SERL Observatory](https://datacatalogue.ukdataservice.ac.uk/studies/study/8666) (UK) | Accredited-researcher application, months | UKDS secure agreement | ~13k homes, half-hourly, 2019–24 | Strong | Web search |
| 7 | [Smart-Grid Smart-City](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/smart-grid-smart-city-customer-trial-data) (Australia) | Open download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | Half-hourly, 2010–14 | Partial | Today |
| 8 | [Ausgrid Solar Home](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/nsw-solar-home-electricty-data) | Open, but see caveat | Not checked | 300 homes, half-hourly, 3 years | Partial | Today |
| 9 | [Pecan Street Dataport](https://www.pecanstreet.org/dataport/) (US) | Free academic account, days | Non-commercial only | 75 homes in the free tier, 1-min | Partial | Today |

## Details
**GoiEner** (start here for scale)
- Hourly kWh for a Spanish energy cooperative's customers, 2014-11 to 2022-06.
- It comes as raw (2 GB) and imputed files (about 0.5–0.8 GB each), plus a metadata file. The imputed files only include series longer than a year, which covers your R2.
- It includes some small businesses, farms and industrial tariffs. Filter on the tariff column (2.X is households and small businesses).
- The Zenodo page says CC-BY-SA in the description but CC BY 4.0 in the rights field. Cite the WHY project.
- The 2020–21 pandemic period is in the data.

**Low Carbon London**
- This is the most-used UK benchmark: a representative sample, about 167 million rows and 10 GB unzipped.
- About 1,100 households were on dynamic time-of-use prices in 2013, with the price given a day ahead. Treat them as a confound for day-ahead forecasting, or exclude them.
- Many households have less than the full 2.3 years.
- A published day-ahead benchmark used 71 filtered households from it: [Benchmarking of Load Forecasting Methods Using Residential Smart Meter Data](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12199844) (2022).

**Irish CER trial**
- Download the Research Purposes request form from the ISSDA page, sign it and email it to issda@ucd.ie.
- It runs about 1.5 years (from memory) and mixes homes and businesses, so you'll need to filter to residential.
- Don't use unofficial mirrors. A ScienceDB copy turned up in the registry, and I didn't verify its permissions.

**SERL**
- You need Accredited Researcher status through the ONS accreditation service (every team member), the UKDS Secure Access agreement and approval from the SERL governance board.
- You can't download the data, and you must be in the UK when you access it.
- SERL's site quotes 4–6 weeks for review. The UKDS guidance says 3–4 months overall.
- There is also a [safeguarded SERL statistical dataset](https://doi.org/10.5255/ukda-sn-8963-1), but it is aggregated, so it doesn't meet your per-household requirement.

**Pecan Street**
- The free tier covers 75 homes (25 each in Austin, New York and California) for non-commercial academic use.
- Paid university tiers start at $10,000. That is too small for "hundreds of households" unless you pay.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** the page confirms half-hourly data for the trial, but I haven't checked per-customer history. I recall about 13,000 customers, and many have partial coverage.
- **Ausgrid Solar Home:** all 300 homes have rooftop PV, so consumption and generation are separate channels. One user's quality screening kept only about 54 homes. Ausgrid's own page returned 404 to my tool. Check whether the data.gov.au link still serves the files.
- **iFlex:** it spans about two winters (early 2020 to spring 2021), so many households may have under a year. Check the lengths.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Electricity Load Diagrams (UCI, 370 clients):** Portuguese clients that are mostly commercial or industrial, not homes. It is a common forecasting benchmark, so it's easy to mistake for household data.
- **UK-DALE, REFIT, ECO:** these are household datasets, but they have only about 5 to 20 homes each (from memory).
- **Synthetic sets (ResStock-based, LPG-derived, OpenSynth):** simulated rather than measured.

## What I could not find
- I did not check per-household history length inside any dataset. For R2, count households with at least 365 days after you download.
- I found no other open household set at this scale with 1+ years of hourly data. The registry search also returned many small or country-specific sets that I did not review individually.

- I did not check per-household history length inside any dataset. For R2, count households with at least 365 days after you download.
- I found no other open household set at this scale with 1+ years of hourly data. The registry search also returned many small or country-specific sets that I did not review individually.

## Details
**GoiEner** (start here for scale)
- Hourly kWh for a Spanish energy cooperative's customers, 2014-11 to 2022-06.
- It comes as raw (2 GB) and imputed files (about 0.5–0.8 GB each), plus a metadata file. The imputed files only include series longer than a year, which covers your R2.
- It includes some small businesses, farms and industrial tariffs. Filter on the tariff column (2.X is households and small businesses).
- The Zenodo page says CC-BY-SA in the description but CC BY 4.0 in the rights field. Cite the WHY project.
- The 2020–21 pandemic period is in the data.

**Low Carbon London**
- This is the most-used UK benchmark: a representative sample, about 167 million rows and 10 GB unzipped.
- About 1,100 households were on dynamic time-of-use prices in 2013, with the price given a day ahead. Treat them as a confound for day-ahead forecasting, or exclude them.
- Many households have less than the full 2.3 years.
- A published day-ahead benchmark used 71 filtered households from it: [Benchmarking of Load Forecasting Methods Using Residential Smart Meter Data](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12199844) (2022).

**Irish CER trial**
- Download the Research Purposes request form from the ISSDA page, sign it and email it to issda@ucd.ie.
- It runs about 1.5 years (from memory) and mixes homes and businesses, so you'll need to filter to residential.
- Don't use unofficial mirrors. A ScienceDB copy turned up in the registry, and I didn't verify its permissions.

**SERL**
- You need Accredited Researcher status through the ONS accreditation service (every team member), the UKDS Secure Access agreement and approval from the SERL governance board.
- You can't download the data, and you must be in the UK when you access it.
- SERL's site quotes 4–6 weeks for review. The UKDS guidance says 3–4 months overall.
- There is also a [safeguarded SERL statistical dataset](https://doi.org/10.5255/ukda-sn-8963-1), but it is aggregated, so it doesn't meet your per-household requirement.

**Pecan Street**
- The free tier covers 75 homes (25 each in Austin, New York and California) for non-commercial academic use.
- Paid university tiers start at $10,000. That is too small for "hundreds of households" unless you pay.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** the page confirms half-hourly data for the trial, but I haven't checked per-customer history. I recall about 13,000 customers, and many have partial coverage.
- **Ausgrid Solar Home:** all 300 homes have rooftop PV, so consumption and generation are separate channels. One user's quality screening kept only about 54 homes. Ausgrid's own page returned 404 to my tool. Check whether the data.gov.au link still serves the files.
- **iFlex:** it spans about two winters (early 2020 to spring 2021), so many households may have under a year. Check the lengths.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Electricity Load Diagrams (UCI, 370 clients):** Portuguese clients that are mostly commercial or industrial, not homes. It is a common forecasting benchmark, so it's easy to mistake for household data.
- **UK-DALE, REFIT, ECO:** these are household datasets, but they have only about 5 to 20 homes each (from memory).
- **Synthetic sets (ResStock-based, LPG-derived, OpenSynth):** simulated rather than measured.

## What I could not find
- I did not check per-household history length inside any dataset. For R2, count households with at least 365 days after you download.
- I found no other open household set at this scale with 1+ years of hourly data. The registry search also returned many small or country-specific sets that I did not review individually.

## Next step
Download GoiEner (filtered to household tariffs) and Low Carbon London (excluding the time-of-use group) now, and send the ISSDA form in parallel since it takes days. Only start the SERL accreditation if you need UK data from after 2014, because it takes months.
