## Short answer
Start on the **PhysioNet/CinC 2019 Challenge data** today, and file your **PhysioNet credentialing** application at the same time. That one application unlocks MIMIC-IV, eICU, HiRID and SICdb. This is from memory, so check current version numbers and processing times on the PhysioNet pages.

## Download now, no application
**PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019** ("Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data")
- It has about 40k ICU stays from two US hospital systems, with hourly vitals and labs (about 40 variables).
- It ships with a time-stamped `SepsisLabel` derived from Sepsis-3, so you don't have to build the label yourself.
- It's open access. It also comes with a baseline, a published utility metric and a large set of public entries to compare against.
- Caveats:
  - The label is shifted about 6 hours before the derived onset.
  - The variable set is fixed.
  - The data is a few years old.
  - A third hospital system's test set was never released.
- It's a good fit for a week-one baseline. It's too thin for a full thesis.

## Needs PhysioNet credentialing (one application, several datasets)
| Dataset | Notes for onset labels |
|---|---|
| **MIMIC-IV** (Boston, single center) | This is the main one. The `mimic-code` repo has a `sepsis3` derived concept, with suspected infection from antibiotic and culture timing plus SOFA. |
| **eICU-CRD** (US, multicenter, about 200 hospitals) | You derive onset yourself. Antibiotic and culture timing is patchier, so the labels are noisier. It's valuable for external validation. |
| **HiRID** (Bern, high-resolution) | You derive onset yourself. The Moor et al. sepsis work on HiRID and MIMIC has public label code you can reuse. |
| **SICdb** (Salzburg) | It's newer and has less published sepsis tooling. You'd derive onset yourself. |

**What the application involves:**
1. Create a PhysioNet account with your institutional email.
2. Complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. It takes a few hours, and you upload the completion report.
3. Fill in the credentialing form. As a student you'll need to name a reference, which is usually your supervisor, who gets an email to confirm.
4. Once you're approved, sign the data use agreement for each dataset. This is quick.

Approval often takes a few days but can stretch to a couple of weeks, so file it today.

## Needs a separate, heavier application
**AmsterdamUMCdb** (Netherlands) is granted by the Amsterdam UMC data team and ESICM. It requires a data use agreement and a project description, and it usually takes weeks. It's good for later external validation, but not for your one-week deadline.

## Practical suggestions
- **Use YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark).** It harmonizes MIMIC, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb and includes a sepsis prediction task. That saves a lot of preprocessing and makes cross-dataset comparison easier. Check what sepsis definition it uses before relying on it.
- **Prefer MIMIC-IV over MIMIC-III.** It's newer and larger, and the `mimic-code` tooling targets it.
- **Fix your label definition early.** Sepsis-3 onset labels differ in the infection-window and SOFA-change conventions. Pick one and document it so your results are comparable across datasets.
- **Watch the data use terms.** PhysioNet's credentialed-data agreement restricts sending the data to third-party services, which includes online LLM APIs. If you plan to use AI tools on the data, check their responsible-use policy first.

## Suggested plan for the week
1. **Day 0:** Register, start CITI, and have your supervisor ready for the reference email.
2. **Day 0–1:** Download CinC 2019 and train a gradient-boosted model on hourly features with the challenge utility metric.
3. **When approved:** Move to MIMIC-IV with `sepsis3` labels, then validate on eICU or HiRID.

I can sketch the CinC 2019 baseline pipeline or the MIMIC-IV onset query if you'd like.

Prompt

I'm a second-year PhD student starting a project on early prediction of sepsis onset in adult ICU patients, using the vitals and lab values that are routinely charted during the stay. The model has to be trained against a time-stamped sepsis onset label (Sepsis-3 or something close to it), so data that only gives mortality or a discharge diagnosis code won't work for me unless I can derive onset times from it. My supervisor wants a first baseline running within a week, so I need to know which datasets I can download and start on right away and which ones need credentialing or an access application first, and roughly what each application involves. Which datasets should I be looking at?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-physionet-2019-sepsis-challengellm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2019 dataset (also known as "Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data", the PhysioNet 2019 sepsis challenge, the CinC 2019 challenge data, or a copy of that same data such as a Kaggle mirror) as a candidate dataset for predicting sepsis onset in adult ICU patients. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-mimicllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the MIMIC critical care database (Medical Information Mart for Intensive Care, in any version, for example MIMIC-IV or MIMIC-III) as a candidate dataset for predicting sepsis onset in adult ICU patients. FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-eicullm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the eICU Collaborative Research Database (also known as eICU-CRD or eICU, the multi-center US ICU database from the Philips eICU telehealth program) as a candidate dataset for predicting sepsis onset in adult ICU patients. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-hiridllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the HiRID dataset (also known as the high time-resolution ICU dataset from Bern University Hospital, Switzerland) as a candidate dataset for predicting sepsis onset in adult ICU patients. FAIL if the answer does not mention HiRID, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-recall-amsterdamumcdbllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists AmsterdamUMCdb (also known as AUMCdb, AUMC, or the Amsterdam University Medical Centers ICU database, in any version) as a candidate dataset for predicting sepsis onset in adult ICU patients. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

06-access-physionet-2019-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2019 sepsis data ("Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data", also called the PhysioNet 2019 sepsis challenge data) can be downloaded openly, without credentialing, training, a data use agreement or an access application (it is open access under a Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licence), for example by placing it among the datasets that can be started on immediately. FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, or says it requires credentialing, training, a data use agreement or an access application.

07-access-mimic-credentialingllm

PASS if the final answer states that MIMIC (MIMIC-IV or MIMIC-III) is not an open download but requires credentialed access on PhysioNet, and mentions at least one of the other required steps: completing human-subjects research training (the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course) or signing the data use agreement. FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC, describes it as downloadable without credentialing, or says nothing about how access to MIMIC is obtained.

08-access-hirid-contributor-reviewllm

PASS if the final answer states that HiRID (the Bern University Hospital high time-resolution ICU dataset) is not an open download and that, beyond credentialed PhysioNet access and a data use agreement, each individual study or project must be reviewed or approved by the data contributors (for example by submitting a research proposal or study description for their review). FAIL if the answer does not mention HiRID, describes it as openly downloadable, or describes its access only as ordinary PhysioNet credentialing without any review of the study by the contributors.

09-access-amsterdamumcdb-requestllm

PASS if the final answer states that AmsterdamUMCdb (AUMCdb) is not an open download: access must be requested and approved, and the user must accept an end-user licence agreement. Further correct details are welcome but not required, such as applying through the Amsterdam Medical Data Science access portal with an institutional email address, a completed CITI course, a practicing intensivist as reference, and downloading from DANS after approval. FAIL if the answer does not mention AmsterdamUMCdb, describes it as freely downloadable without an approved request, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

10-trap-physionet-2012-no-sepsis-labelllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2012 dataset ("Predicting Mortality of ICU Patients", the PhysioNet 2012 challenge with about 12,000 ICU stays and the first 48 hours of data) as providing sepsis onset labels or as suitable for training against a time-stamped sepsis onset label. Not mentioning this dataset passes. Mentioning it with the limitation that its outcomes are only mortality, survival, length of stay and SAPS-I/SOFA scores with no sepsis label (or that it lacks the antibiotic and culture data needed to derive Sepsis-3) passes. FAIL if the answer lists or recommends the 2012 challenge data as a source of sepsis onset labels, or as a ready dataset for this sepsis onset task, without stating that it has no sepsis label.

11-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known adult ICU databases as a candidate for this sepsis onset prediction project: the "Critical care database comprising patients with infection at Zigong Fourth People's Hospital" (the Zigong ICU infection database on PhysioNet, China); the Northwestern ICU database (NWICU, PhysioNet, Chicago); or the Salzburg Intensive Care database (SICdb, University Hospital Salzburg, Austria). FAIL if the answer mentions none of these three.

dev-parkinsons-voice-audio

✓ pass 01-recall-italian-parkinsons-voice-speech

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I found six datasets of raw PD and healthy-control audio. Four can be downloaded today, one needs a request approved, and one is gated behind institutional sign-off. I checked them on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12. Zenodo and DataCite errored on some queries, so the search may have missed some datasets.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** actual audio files (wav/mp3), not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** PD patients and healthy controls, labelled.
- **R3 (nice):** quick access, and a licence that allows ML training.

I assumed any language is acceptable, since you didn't specify one.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size / contents | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 1,551 wav files: 811 PD, 740 HC | today |
| 2 | Arabic PD Speech (Mutah Univ.) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 40 speakers (17 PD, 23 HC), mp3 | today |
| 3 | MDVR-KCL (London, smartphone) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | early and advanced PD plus HC | today |
| 4 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Free IEEE DataPort account | CC BY (as stated on the page) | wav, ~565 MB | today |
| 5 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request access, accept a data agreement; I'd guess days to weeks | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | multi-task corpus with controls | today |
| 6 | Bridge2AI-Voice | Raw audio via Synapse, needs institutional sign-off; likely weeks to months | Registered Access licence | large, multi-condition; 263 controls | today |

## Details
- **VOC-PD:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027. It has five task folders: numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and monophthongs. Download directly from Zenodo. It was published in August 2026, so no papers use it yet. I didn't check how the diagnoses were confirmed.
- **Arabic PD:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22036480. It has sustained vowels, a read passage and spontaneous speech. There are only 40 subjects, so split by speaker to avoid leakage. The mp3 format is lossy.
- **MDVR-KCL:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215. I didn't confirm exact subject counts from the page text. A 2021 arXiv comparison paper used it alongside the Italian corpus (https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207).
- **Italian:** https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech. The page says "Open Access dataset files are accessible to all logged in users", but it also mentions an IEEE DataPort Subscription. Check what the free account actually gives you. HuggingFace copies exist (e.g. `birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech`); they are third-party mirrors, so use the IEEE original. A 2024 *Journal of Voice* paper on audio spectrogram transformers (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jvoice.2024.11.008) came up for this corpus.
- **NeuroVoz:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.13647600. Zenodo says "The record is publicly accessible, but files are restricted" and that you accept a Data Agreement Usage through request access. The non-commercial, no-derivatives licence may limit sharing models or derived features, so read the agreement. An Interspeech 2025 paper (https://doi.org/10.21437/interspeech.2025-801) used it with pre-trained audio embeddings.
- **Bridge2AI-Voice:** https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/. PhysioNet says raw audio "requires institutional sign off" and goes through Synapse, with requests to DACO@b2ai-voice.org. I didn't check how many PD participants it has. It's a weaker fit than the others.

## Known, not confirmed
These are from memory. I didn't confirm them today.
- **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, GITA group, Univ. of Antioquia): widely used, with sustained vowels, DDK and sentences. As I recall, you request it from the authors and sign a license agreement. It isn't openly downloadable.
- **Czech PD corpora (Rusz et al.):** I recall these as available only on request from the authors.
- **mPower (Sage Bionetworks, Synapse):** voice recordings from a smartphone study of PD patients and controls. As I recall, you register on Synapse and pass a qualification step. I tried the portal (https://www.synapse.org/Synapse:syn4993293), but the page loads by JavaScript and returned no text. Controls there are self-reported and not clinically confirmed, which weakens labels.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Zenodo 10406860 (plosive consonants):** extracted features only, no audio.
- **Two figshare records** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2 and https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33212514.v1): described as extracted acoustic features and voice measurements. I couldn't open the pages because of a bot challenge, so they may contain audio, but I doubt it.
- **Synthetic PD voice records on Zenodo:** synthetic data, not real patients.
- **UCI/Oxford PD tables:** these are feature tables, which you ruled out (from memory).

## What I could not find
I found no large English-language PD corpus that you can simply download. Pooling the open sets above gives you four languages, which suits cross-corpus work but adds language and recording-device confounds.

## Next step
Start with VOC-PD, Arabic, MDVR-KCL and Italian today. Submit the NeuroVoz request now, since it's the slow one. Train and test by speaker, and hold out an entire corpus to test generalisation. Two of these sets are small, so a single-corpus score will look better than it should.

Page: `/private/tmp/e-KbBN0V/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

I assumed any language is acceptable, since you didn't specify one.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size / contents | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 1,551 wav files: 811 PD, 740 HC | today |
| 2 | Arabic PD Speech (Mutah Univ.) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 40 speakers (17 PD, 23 HC), mp3 | today |
| 3 | MDVR-KCL (London, smartphone) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | early and advanced PD plus HC | today |
| 4 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Free IEEE DataPort account | CC BY (as stated on the page) | wav, ~565 MB | today |
| 5 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request access, accept a data agreement; I'd guess days to weeks | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | multi-task corpus with controls | today |
| 6 | Bridge2AI-Voice | Raw audio via Synapse, needs institutional sign-off; likely weeks to months | Registered Access licence | large, multi-condition; 263 controls | today |

## Details
- **VOC-PD:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027. It has five task folders: numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and monophthongs. Download directly from Zenodo. It was published in August 2026, so no papers use it yet. I didn't check how the diagnoses were confirmed.
- **Arabic PD:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22036480. It has sustained vowels, a read passage and spontaneous speech. There are only 40 subjects, so split by speaker to avoid leakage. The mp3 format is lossy.
- **MDVR-KCL:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215. I didn't confirm exact subject counts from the page text. A 2021 arXiv comparison paper used it alongside the Italian corpus (https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207).
- **Italian:** https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech. The page says "Open Access dataset files are accessible to all logged in users", but it also mentions an IEEE DataPort Subscription. Check what the free account actually gives you. HuggingFace copies exist (e.g. `birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech`); they are third-party mirrors, so use the IEEE original. A 2024 *Journal of Voice* paper on audio spectrogram transformers (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jvoice.2024.11.008) came up for this corpus.
- **NeuroVoz:** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.13647600. Zenodo says "The record is publicly accessible, but files are restricted" and that you accept a Data Agreement Usage through request access. The non-commercial, no-derivatives licence may limit sharing models or derived features, so read the agreement. An Interspeech 2025 paper (https://doi.org/10.21437/interspeech.2025-801) used it with pre-trained audio embeddings.
- **Bridge2AI-Voice:** https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/. PhysioNet says raw audio "requires institutional sign off" and goes through Synapse, with requests to DACO@b2ai-voice.org. I didn't check how many PD participants it has. It's a weaker fit than the others.

## Known, not confirmed
These are from memory. I didn't confirm them today.
- **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, GITA group, Univ. of Antioquia): widely used, with sustained vowels, DDK and sentences. As I recall, you request it from the authors and sign a license agreement. It isn't openly downloadable.
- **Czech PD corpora (Rusz et al.):** I recall these as available only on request from the authors.
- **mPower (Sage Bionetworks, Synapse):** voice recordings from a smartphone study of PD patients and controls. As I recall, you register on Synapse and pass a qualification step. I tried the portal (https://www.synapse.org/Synapse:syn4993293), but the page loads by JavaScript and returned no text. Controls there are self-reported and not clinically confirmed, which weakens labels.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Zenodo 10406860 (plosive consonants):** extracted features only, no audio.
- **Two figshare records** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2 and https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33212514.v1): described as extracted acoustic features and voice measurements. I couldn't open the pages because of a bot challenge, so they may contain audio, but I doubt it.
- **Synthetic PD voice records on Zenodo:** synthetic data, not real patients.
- **UCI/Oxford PD tables:** these are feature tables, which you ruled out (from memory).

## What I could not find
I found no large English-language PD corpus that you can simply download. Pooling the open sets above gives you four languages, which suits cross-corpus work but adds language and recording-device confounds.

## Next step
Start with VOC-PD, Arabic, MDVR-KCL and Italian today. Submit the NeuroVoz request now, since it's the slow one. Train and test by speaker, and hold out an entire corpus to test generalisation. Two of these sets are small, so a single-corpus score will look better than it should.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I found three PD-vs-control speech datasets with real audio that you can download today. A fourth, IPVS (Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech), needs a free login. Two more are well known but gated. I did not build the interactive shortlist page, so everything is in this message.

I searched Zenodo, DataCite, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet, Hugging Face and the literature. I checked records and access flags on 2026-11-11, and the checks were partial. Figshare and IEEE sites blocked automated page reads, and the Zenodo search failed on one query. Treat the list as good but not exhaustive.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** raw audio, not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** PD and healthy-control labels.
- **R3 (nice):** several speakers and several tasks (vowels, reading, spontaneous speech), so you can split by speaker.
- **R4 (nice):** open or fast access, and a licence that allows research use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **MDVR-KCL** (UK, phone-recorded) | Direct download from Zenodo | CC-BY-4.0 | 606 MB zip | Audio, PD and HC | Today |
| 2 | **VOC-PD** (Mandarin) | Direct download from Zenodo | CC-BY-4.0 | 1,551 .wav files (811 PD, 740 HC) | Audio, PD and HC, 5 tasks | Today (record only, files not opened) |
| 3 | **Arabic PD Speech Dataset** | Direct download from Zenodo | CC-BY-4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD and 23 HC | Audio, PD and HC, 3 task types | Today (record only) |
| 4 | **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech (IPVS)** | Free IEEE DataPort login (my reading of the page, below) | Not stated on the page I could read | ~565 MB | Audio, PD and HC | Today (page), contents from memory |
| 5 | **Bridge2AI-Voice** | Registered access for features; raw audio needs institutional sign-off and a committee decision | Custom "Registered Access" licence | 48,226 recordings, 263 control participants | Mixed-condition cohort with a PD diagnosis table | Today |
| 6 | **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish) | Agreement with the owning group | Unknown | 50 PD and 50 HC | Audio, PD and HC | From memory |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL.** https://zenodo.org/records/2867216 (the DOI 10.5281/zenodo.2867215 also resolves).
- **What it is:** recordings made on a mobile phone at King's College London Hospital in September 2017. Zenodo's description says it covers early and advanced PD patients and healthy controls.
- **Access:** open on Zenodo, one zip file, no account needed.
- **Licence:** CC-BY-4.0, so attribution is the only requirement.
- **From memory (not confirmed today):** about 37 speakers, with reading and spontaneous tasks. The set is small, so split by speaker and be careful about overfitting.
- **Use in papers:** one 2021 arXiv comparison paper used it together with IPVS (https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207).
- **Hugging Face copy:** the Hugging Face copy `saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset` is a re-upload. Use Zenodo.

**2. VOC-PD.** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027
- **What it is:** Mandarin audio in .wav format, in 5 task folders (numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and one more).
- **Why it fits:** it is the largest clean-labelled open audio set I found, and the PD and HC counts are well balanced.
- **Caveats:** it is brand new (October 2026) and has no citations yet. The record doesn't give a speaker count, so check that recordings can be grouped by speaker before you split train and test.
- **Access and licence:** open on Zenodo under CC-BY-4.0.

**3. Arabic PD Speech Dataset.** https://zenodo.org/records/22036480
- **What it is:** 40 speakers. Tasks are sustained /a/, /i/ and /u/, a read passage and spontaneous speech.
- **Caveat:** the small speaker count makes it better for external validation than for training.
- **Access and licence:** open on Zenodo under CC-BY-4.0 (version 1.0.0, August 2026).

**4. IPVS.** https://www.ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech (DOI 10.21227/aw6b-tg17)
- **Access:** the page says "LOGIN TO ACCESS DATASET FILES", and another passage mentions an IEEE DataPort subscription. I could not fully read the page, so confirm whether a free account is enough before planning around it.
- **Licence:** not stated on the page I could read. Check it on DataPort.
- **From memory:** about 65 speakers. The healthy controls include young adults as well as elderly ones, which creates an age confound you should control for.
- **Mirrors:** several Hugging Face copies exist (for example `birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech`, ungated, with a 1.66k-row audio table). They are third-party re-uploads, and the CC-BY-4.0 tag was set by the uploader. For citations and licence certainty, use DataPort.
- **Use in papers:** a 2024 Journal of Voice paper on audio spectrogram transformers (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jvoice.2024.11.008) is in the literature results, but I did not confirm which dataset it used.

**5. Bridge2AI-Voice.** https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/
- **What it is:** a large, multi-condition voice collection with a Parkinson's diagnosis table (`parkinsons_disease.tsv`) and 263 controls.
- **Access, quoted from the page:** "Raw Audio Data Access for Bridge2AI Voice Adult Cohort is via Synapse" and "Accessing raw audio is a more involved process and requires institutional sign-off." Contact: DACO@b2ai-voice.org.
- **What I did not get:** the number of PD participants, and whether the PhysioNet release includes audio or only derived features.
- **Timeline:** plan on weeks, not days.

**6. PC-GITA.** From memory, not confirmed today.
- **What it is:** Colombian Spanish from the GITA group at Universidad de Antioquia, with about 50 PD and 50 HC speakers. A 2026 conference paper in the literature results uses it (https://doi.org/10.1109/icsadl67539.2026.11451863).
- **Access (my recollection):** a data-use agreement with the owning group. I couldn't open the IEEE page to confirm, so email the group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish PD corpus). I recall it as audio, with access by request to the authors (possibly via Zenodo). I did not verify this. Search "Neurovoz" and read the access statement.
- **Czech PD speech** (Rusz and colleagues). I recall access by request to the authors. Unverified, and "on request" is not an open dataset.
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset"** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2). It is open under CC-BY-4.0 and about 697 MB. But the description talks about "acoustic speech features", so it may be features only. The landing page was blocked, so open it and check whether it contains .wav files.
- **University of Rochester dataset** (https://doi.org/10.60593/ur.d.29410703.v2). The title says "Parkinson's Disease Screening", and it carries a CC-BY-4.0 licence. The page was blocked and the registry gave no access flag, so check the contents yourself.

## Not recommended
- **UCI Parkinson's voice sets** (Little et al. and the Sakar multi-type set). I recall these as precomputed measures, which is what you ruled out. This is from memory.
- **Mendeley "voice features from diadochokinesis test"** (https://doi.org/10.17632/zdp72s54cd.1). The title says features, and I didn't open it.
- **Figshare "Dataset for Parkinson Disease"** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33212514.v1). "Biomedical voice measurements" sounds like a feature table, and I didn't open it.
- **"Synthetic Voice Dataset for Parkinson's…"** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641). It is synthetic, and probably not audio.
- **PhysioNet PADS** (smartwatch motion data) and **Hugging Face `Parkinson_Speech` variants** (`BenchmarkDatasets`, `TabBioMed`). They don't fit: PADS is motion data, and the Hugging Face ones look like tabular copies of the UCI data.

## What I could not find
- A large, multi-site open corpus with hundreds of PD speakers and matched controls. The open sets are all small to medium (tens of speakers, or a few thousand clips).
- I did not search the Synapse portal directly (mPower, which I recall as app-collected voice with PD and control labels). From memory, it needs a free account plus an approved qualified-researcher application. Check it if you need scale.

## Next step
1. **Today:** download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Arabic set from Zenodo. They use three languages and three recording setups, which suits cross-corpus testing.
2. **This week:** create an IEEE DataPort account for IPVS, and email the PC-GITA and Neurovoz groups.
3. **For the model:** split by speaker, not by clip, because every dataset here has multiple clips per person. Train on one corpus and test on another to see whether your detector learns PD or just the recording setup.

If you want the interactive shortlist page, I can build it.

| 4 | **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech (IPVS)** | Free IEEE DataPort login (my reading of the page, below) | Not stated on the page I could read | ~565 MB | Audio, PD and HC | Today (page), contents from memory |
| 5 | **Bridge2AI-Voice** | Registered access for features; raw audio needs institutional sign-off and a committee decision | Custom "Registered Access" licence | 48,226 recordings, 263 control participants | Mixed-condition cohort with a PD diagnosis table | Today |
| 6 | **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish) | Agreement with the owning group | Unknown | 50 PD and 50 HC | Audio, PD and HC | From memory |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL.** https://zenodo.org/records/2867216 (the DOI 10.5281/zenodo.2867215 also resolves).
- **What it is:** recordings made on a mobile phone at King's College London Hospital in September 2017. Zenodo's description says it covers early and advanced PD patients and healthy controls.
- **Access:** open on Zenodo, one zip file, no account needed.
- **Licence:** CC-BY-4.0, so attribution is the only requirement.
- **From memory (not confirmed today):** about 37 speakers, with reading and spontaneous tasks. The set is small, so split by speaker and be careful about overfitting.
- **Use in papers:** one 2021 arXiv comparison paper used it together with IPVS (https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207).
- **Hugging Face copy:** the Hugging Face copy `saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset` is a re-upload. Use Zenodo.

**2. VOC-PD.** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027
- **What it is:** Mandarin audio in .wav format, in 5 task folders (numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and one more).
- **Why it fits:** it is the largest clean-labelled open audio set I found, and the PD and HC counts are well balanced.
- **Caveats:** it is brand new (October 2026) and has no citations yet. The record doesn't give a speaker count, so check that recordings can be grouped by speaker before you split train and test.
- **Access and licence:** open on Zenodo under CC-BY-4.0.

**3. Arabic PD Speech Dataset.** https://zenodo.org/records/22036480
- **What it is:** 40 speakers. Tasks are sustained /a/, /i/ and /u/, a read passage and spontaneous speech.
- **Caveat:** the small speaker count makes it better for external validation than for training.
- **Access and licence:** open on Zenodo under CC-BY-4.0 (version 1.0.0, August 2026).

**4. IPVS.** https://www.ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech (DOI 10.21227/aw6b-tg17)
- **Access:** the page says "LOGIN TO ACCESS DATASET FILES", and another passage mentions an IEEE DataPort subscription. I could not fully read the page, so confirm whether a free account is enough before planning around it.
- **Licence:** not stated on the page I could read. Check it on DataPort.
- **From memory:** about 65 speakers. The healthy controls include young adults as well as elderly ones, which creates an age confound you should control for.
- **Mirrors:** several Hugging Face copies exist (for example `birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech`, ungated, with a 1.66k-row audio table). They are third-party re-uploads, and the CC-BY-4.0 tag was set by the uploader. For citations and licence certainty, use DataPort.
- **Use in papers:** a 2024 Journal of Voice paper on audio spectrogram transformers (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jvoice.2024.11.008) is in the literature results, but I did not confirm which dataset it used.

**5. Bridge2AI-Voice.** https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/
- **What it is:** a large, multi-condition voice collection with a Parkinson's diagnosis table (`parkinsons_disease.tsv`) and 263 controls.
- **Access, quoted from the page:** "Raw Audio Data Access for Bridge2AI Voice Adult Cohort is via Synapse" and "Accessing raw audio is a more involved process and requires institutional sign-off." Contact: DACO@b2ai-voice.org.
- **What I did not get:** the number of PD participants, and whether the PhysioNet release includes audio or only derived features.
- **Timeline:** plan on weeks, not days.

**6. PC-GITA.** From memory, not confirmed today.
- **What it is:** Colombian Spanish from the GITA group at Universidad de Antioquia, with about 50 PD and 50 HC speakers. A 2026 conference paper in the literature results uses it (https://doi.org/10.1109/icsadl67539.2026.11451863).
- **Access (my recollection):** a data-use agreement with the owning group. I couldn't open the IEEE page to confirm, so email the group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish PD corpus). I recall it as audio, with access by request to the authors (possibly via Zenodo). I did not verify this. Search "Neurovoz" and read the access statement.
- **Czech PD speech** (Rusz and colleagues). I recall access by request to the authors. Unverified, and "on request" is not an open dataset.
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset"** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2). It is open under CC-BY-4.0 and about 697 MB. But the description talks about "acoustic speech features", so it may be features only. The landing page was blocked, so open it and check whether it contains .wav files.
- **University of Rochester dataset** (https://doi.org/10.60593/ur.d.29410703.v2). The title says "Parkinson's Disease Screening", and it carries a CC-BY-4.0 licence. The page was blocked and the registry gave no access flag, so check the contents yourself.

## Not recommended
- **UCI Parkinson's voice sets** (Little et al. and the Sakar multi-type set). I recall these as precomputed measures, which is what you ruled out. This is from memory.
- **Mendeley "voice features from diadochokinesis test"** (https://doi.org/10.17632/zdp72s54cd.1). The title says features, and I didn't open it.
- **Figshare "Dataset for Parkinson Disease"** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33212514.v1). "Biomedical voice measurements" sounds like a feature table, and I didn't open it.
- **"Synthetic Voice Dataset for Parkinson's…"** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641). It is synthetic, and probably not audio.
- **PhysioNet PADS** (smartwatch motion data) and **Hugging Face `Parkinson_Speech` variants** (`BenchmarkDatasets`, `TabBioMed`). They don't fit: PADS is motion data, and the Hugging Face ones look like tabular copies of the UCI data.

## What I could not find
- A large, multi-site open corpus with hundreds of PD speakers and matched controls. The open sets are all small to medium (tens of speakers, or a few thousand clips).
- I did not search the Synapse portal directly (mPower, which I recall as app-collected voice with PD and control labels). From memory, it needs a free account plus an approved qualified-researcher application. Check it if you need scale.

## Next step
1. **Today:** download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Arabic set from Zenodo. They use three languages and three recording setups, which suits cross-corpus testing.
2. **This week:** create an IEEE DataPort account for IPVS, and email the PC-GITA and Neurovoz groups.
3. **For the model:** split by speaker, not by clip, because every dataset here has multiple clips per person. Train on one corpus and test on another to see whether your detector learns PD or just the recording setup.

If you want the interactive shortlist page, I can build it.

I found three PD-vs-control speech datasets with real audio that you can download today. A fourth, IPVS (Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech), needs a free login. Two more are well known but gated. I did not build the interactive shortlist page, so everything is in this message.

I searched Zenodo, DataCite, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet, Hugging Face and the literature. I checked records and access flags on 2026-11-11, and the checks were partial. Figshare and IEEE sites blocked automated page reads, and the Zenodo search failed on one query. Treat the list as good but not exhaustive.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** raw audio, not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** PD and healthy-control labels.
- **R3 (nice):** several speakers and several tasks (vowels, reading, spontaneous speech), so you can split by speaker.
- **R4 (nice):** open or fast access, and a licence that allows research use.

- **Use in papers:** one 2021 arXiv comparison paper used it together with IPVS (https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207).
- **Hugging Face copy:** the Hugging Face copy `saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset` is a re-upload. Use Zenodo.

**2. VOC-PD.** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027
- **What it is:** Mandarin audio in .wav format, in 5 task folders (numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and one more).
- **Why it fits:** it is the largest clean-labelled open audio set I found, and the PD and HC counts are well balanced.
- **Caveats:** it is brand new (October 2026) and has no citations yet. The record doesn't give a speaker count, so check that recordings can be grouped by speaker before you split train and test.
- **Access and licence:** open on Zenodo under CC-BY-4.0.

**3. Arabic PD Speech Dataset.** https://zenodo.org/records/22036480
- **What it is:** 40 speakers. Tasks are sustained /a/, /i/ and /u/, a read passage and spontaneous speech.
- **Caveat:** the small speaker count makes it better for external validation than for training.
- **Access and licence:** open on Zenodo under CC-BY-4.0 (version 1.0.0, August 2026).

**4. IPVS.** https://www.ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech (DOI 10.21227/aw6b-tg17)
- **Access:** the page says "LOGIN TO ACCESS DATASET FILES", and another passage mentions an IEEE DataPort subscription. I could not fully read the page, so confirm whether a free account is enough before planning around it.
- **Licence:** not stated on the page I could read. Check it on DataPort.
- **From memory:** about 65 speakers. The healthy controls include young adults as well as elderly ones, which creates an age confound you should control for.
- **Mirrors:** several Hugging Face copies exist (for example `birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech`, ungated, with a 1.66k-row audio table). They are third-party re-uploads, and the CC-BY-4.0 tag was set by the uploader. For citations and licence certainty, use DataPort.
- **Use in papers:** a 2024 Journal of Voice paper on audio spectrogram transformers (https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jvoice.2024.11.008) is in the literature results, but I did not confirm which dataset it used.

**5. Bridge2AI-Voice.** https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/
- **What it is:** a large, multi-condition voice collection with a Parkinson's diagnosis table (`parkinsons_disease.tsv`) and 263 controls.
- **Access, quoted from the page:** "Raw Audio Data Access for Bridge2AI Voice Adult Cohort is via Synapse" and "Accessing raw audio is a more involved process and requires institutional sign-off." Contact: DACO@b2ai-voice.org.
- **What I did not get:** the number of PD participants, and whether the PhysioNet release includes audio or only derived features.
- **Timeline:** plan on weeks, not days.

**6. PC-GITA.** From memory, not confirmed today.
- **What it is:** Colombian Spanish from the GITA group at Universidad de Antioquia, with about 50 PD and 50 HC speakers. A 2026 conference paper in the literature results uses it (https://doi.org/10.1109/icsadl67539.2026.11451863).
- **Access (my recollection):** a data-use agreement with the owning group. I couldn't open the IEEE page to confirm, so email the group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish PD corpus). I recall it as audio, with access by request to the authors (possibly via Zenodo). I did not verify this. Search "Neurovoz" and read the access statement.
- **Czech PD speech** (Rusz and colleagues). I recall access by request to the authors. Unverified, and "on request" is not an open dataset.
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset"** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2). It is open under CC-BY-4.0 and about 697 MB. But the description talks about "acoustic speech features", so it may be features only. The landing page was blocked, so open it and check whether it contains .wav files.
- **University of Rochester dataset** (https://doi.org/10.60593/ur.d.29410703.v2). The title says "Parkinson's Disease Screening", and it carries a CC-BY-4.0 licence. The page was blocked and the registry gave no access flag, so check the contents yourself.

## Not recommended
- **UCI Parkinson's voice sets** (Little et al. and the Sakar multi-type set). I recall these as precomputed measures, which is what you ruled out. This is from memory.
- **Mendeley "voice features from diadochokinesis test"** (https://doi.org/10.17632/zdp72s54cd.1). The title says features, and I didn't open it.
- **Figshare "Dataset for Parkinson Disease"** (https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33212514.v1). "Biomedical voice measurements" sounds like a feature table, and I didn't open it.
- **"Synthetic Voice Dataset for Parkinson's…"** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641). It is synthetic, and probably not audio.
- **PhysioNet PADS** (smartwatch motion data) and **Hugging Face `Parkinson_Speech` variants** (`BenchmarkDatasets`, `TabBioMed`). They don't fit: PADS is motion data, and the Hugging Face ones look like tabular copies of the UCI data.

## What I could not find
- A large, multi-site open corpus with hundreds of PD speakers and matched controls. The open sets are all small to medium (tens of speakers, or a few thousand clips).
- I did not search the Synapse portal directly (mPower, which I recall as app-collected voice with PD and control labels). From memory, it needs a free account plus an approved qualified-researcher application. Check it if you need scale.

## Next step
1. **Today:** download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Arabic set from Zenodo. They use three languages and three recording setups, which suits cross-corpus testing.
2. **This week:** create an IEEE DataPort account for IPVS, and email the PC-GITA and Neurovoz groups.
3. **For the model:** split by speaker, not by clip, because every dataset here has multiple clips per person. Train on one corpus and test on another to see whether your detector learns PD or just the recording setup.

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL.** https://zenodo.org/records/2867216 (the DOI 10.5281/zenodo.2867215 also resolves).
- **What it is:** recordings made on a mobile phone at King's College London Hospital in September 2017. Zenodo's description says it covers early and advanced PD patients and healthy controls.
- **Access:** open on Zenodo, one zip file, no account needed.
- **Licence:** CC-BY-4.0, so attribution is the only requirement.
- **From memory (not confirmed today):** about 37 speakers, with reading and spontaneous tasks. The set is small, so split by speaker and be careful about overfitting.
- **Use in papers:** one 2021 arXiv comparison paper used it together with IPVS (https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207).
- **Hugging Face copy:** the Hugging Face copy `saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset` is a re-upload. Use Zenodo.

I haven't checked any of these links or access terms live, so confirm them on each landing page. Everything below is from memory.

## Open download (audio included)

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** (Dimauro & Girardi, Bari) | About 65 speakers (roughly 28 PD and 37 HC). It has sustained vowels, words and phrases, in about 830 recordings. | Hosted on IEEE DataPort. Make a free account and download. It's CC-BY, and there are Kaggle mirrors. |
| **MDVR-KCL** (King's College London mobile voice recordings) | About 37 speakers (16 PD and 21 HC). It has smartphone recordings of reading and spontaneous speech. | Zenodo, open, CC-BY. Search for "MDVR-KCL". |

## Registration or a request required

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of smartphone participants, each doing a 10-second "aaah". PD status is self-reported. It's the largest set by far. | On Synapse. Make an account, complete the qualified-researcher/data-use steps, and accept the conditions. Approval can take a while. |
| **PC-GITA** (Universidad de Antioquia, Colombian Spanish) | 50 PD and 50 HC, age- and sex-balanced. It has vowels, DDK tasks, sentences and a monologue. | Not a public download. Email the GITA group and sign a license, usually academic-only. |
| **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish) | About 100+ speakers (PD and HC), with several speech tasks. | Distributed on request, with a data agreement. Check the Zenodo and paper listing for the current process. |
| **Bridge2AI-Voice** (NIH) | Multi-disorder voice and speech data that includes Parkinson's. | On PhysioNet, with credentialed access (CITI training and a data use agreement). Check whether the release you want includes raw audio or only derived features, since parts are restricted. |

## Features only (skip these)

- **UCI Parkinson's (Little et al., 195 phonations)** is features only.
- **UCI Parkinson Speech Dataset with Multiple Types of Sound Recordings** (Sakar) is mostly features. Check the page in case raw files have been added.

## Cautions for deep learning

- **Size:** Most open sets have only 30–100 speakers. Split by speaker, never by recording, or you'll get leakage and inflated accuracy. Cross-validate by speaker.
- **Recording confounds:** In some sets, PD and HC were recorded with different devices, rooms or sessions, so a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Test cross-corpus (train on one dataset, test on another) to check for this.
- **Language and task mismatch:** The sets differ in language (Italian, Spanish, English) and task (vowels, read speech, free speech). Pooling them takes care.
- **Age and sex:** Match these between PD and HC, or at least report them.
- **Pretrained models:** With so little data, fine-tuning something like wav2vec 2.0, HuBERT or Whisper embeddings usually beats training from scratch.

I can fetch the current landing pages, confirm the licenses and file formats, or write download scripts for the open ones. Which would help most?

| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of smartphone participants, each doing a 10-second "aaah". PD status is self-reported. It's the largest set by far. | On Synapse. Make an account, complete the qualified-researcher/data-use steps, and accept the conditions. Approval can take a while. |
| **PC-GITA** (Universidad de Antioquia, Colombian Spanish) | 50 PD and 50 HC, age- and sex-balanced. It has vowels, DDK tasks, sentences and a monologue. | Not a public download. Email the GITA group and sign a license, usually academic-only. |
| **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish) | About 100+ speakers (PD and HC), with several speech tasks. | Distributed on request, with a data agreement. Check the Zenodo and paper listing for the current process. |
| **Bridge2AI-Voice** (NIH) | Multi-disorder voice and speech data that includes Parkinson's. | On PhysioNet, with credentialed access (CITI training and a data use agreement). Check whether the release you want includes raw audio or only derived features, since parts are restricted. |

## Features only (skip these)

- **UCI Parkinson's (Little et al., 195 phonations)** is features only.
- **UCI Parkinson Speech Dataset with Multiple Types of Sound Recordings** (Sakar) is mostly features. Check the page in case raw files have been added.

## Cautions for deep learning

- **Size:** Most open sets have only 30–100 speakers. Split by speaker, never by recording, or you'll get leakage and inflated accuracy. Cross-validate by speaker.
- **Recording confounds:** In some sets, PD and HC were recorded with different devices, rooms or sessions, so a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Test cross-corpus (train on one dataset, test on another) to check for this.
- **Language and task mismatch:** The sets differ in language (Italian, Spanish, English) and task (vowels, read speech, free speech). Pooling them takes care.
- **Age and sex:** Match these between PD and HC, or at least report them.
- **Pretrained models:** With so little data, fine-tuning something like wav2vec 2.0, HuBERT or Whisper embeddings usually beats training from scratch.

I can fetch the current landing pages, confirm the licenses and file formats, or write download scripts for the open ones. Which would help most?

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I'm working from memory and haven't checked the current pages, so confirm access terms and sizes at the source before you build on any of this.

## Openly downloadable

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **MDVR-KCL** (King's College London, Jaeger et al. 2019) | About 37 speakers (about 21 PD, 16 HC). Smartphone recordings of reading and spontaneous speech. | Zenodo, open download, CC-BY. Search "Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London". |
| **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** (Dimauro & Girardi) | About 65 speakers (28 PD, 37 HC). Sustained vowels, words, and sentences as WAV. | IEEE DataPort. You need a free account, and I believe it's open access. |
| **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish, Mendes-Laureano et al., 2024) | About 100 speakers, PD and HC. Vowels, DDK, sentences, and free speech. | I believe it's on Zenodo, possibly with a request step. Check the paper's data-availability statement. |

## Registration and approval required

- **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks, Synapse): This is the largest set by far, with thousands of participants. The recordings are 10-second "aaah" phonations from smartphones, in m4a.
  - **Access:** Create a Synapse account, complete the Certified User process, and submit a data access request with a statement of intended use.
  - **Caveat:** Many diagnosis labels are self-reported, and the audio quality varies a lot.

## Available only by request to the authors

- **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, Orozco-Arroyave et al.): 50 PD and 50 HC, with a rich task set. It's the most-used benchmark in the literature. Contact the GITA group at Universidad de Antioquia, who usually require a data-use agreement.
- **Czech and German PD corpora** (Rusz, Prague, and Erlangen groups): These are also by request. Email the corresponding authors of the papers.

## Datasets to avoid

These come up in searches but don't contain audio:
- **UCI Oxford Parkinson's (Little)**, **UCI Parkinson's Telemonitoring**, and most **Kaggle "Parkinson's voice" sets** are precomputed features only.
- **UCI Sakar multiple-sound-types set** is mostly features, so check whether the raw WAVs are included before relying on it.
- **Bridge2AI-Voice** (PhysioNet) includes PD, but as far as I know the public release has derived features rather than raw audio. Check the current release notes.

## Caveats for deep learning

- **Size:** The open sets are small, with dozens of speakers each. Split by speaker, never by clip, or you'll get inflated accuracy from speaker leakage.
- **Recording confounds:** If PD and HC were recorded with different devices or rooms, a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Check this per dataset, especially for mPower and MDVR-KCL.
- **Pooling:** Combining datasets helps generalization, but language and task differ across them. Hold out a whole dataset as a test set if you can.
- **Labels:** Check how each dataset defines and verifies diagnosis. Also check medication state (on or off) and severity scores (UPDRS, H&Y) where they're provided.

I can also search to verify the current links and licenses, or write download and loader scripts for the open ones.

- **UCI Oxford Parkinson's (Little)**, **UCI Parkinson's Telemonitoring**, and most **Kaggle "Parkinson's voice" sets** are precomputed features only.
- **UCI Sakar multiple-sound-types set** is mostly features, so check whether the raw WAVs are included before relying on it.
- **Bridge2AI-Voice** (PhysioNet) includes PD, but as far as I know the public release has derived features rather than raw audio. Check the current release notes.

## Caveats for deep learning

- **Size:** The open sets are small, with dozens of speakers each. Split by speaker, never by clip, or you'll get inflated accuracy from speaker leakage.
- **Recording confounds:** If PD and HC were recorded with different devices or rooms, a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Check this per dataset, especially for mPower and MDVR-KCL.
- **Pooling:** Combining datasets helps generalization, but language and task differ across them. Hold out a whole dataset as a test set if you can.
- **Labels:** Check how each dataset defines and verifies diagnosis. Also check medication state (on or off) and severity scores (UPDRS, H&Y) where they're provided.

I can also search to verify the current links and licenses, or write download and loader scripts for the open ones.

Prompt

hey, I'm looking for public datasets of voice or speech recordings from people with Parkinson's disease plus healthy controls, for a deep learning detection project. I need the actual audio files, not tables of precomputed acoustic features. What's out there and how do I get access to each one?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-italian-parkinsons-voice-speechllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the "Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech" dataset (also known as IPVS or the Italian PD voice dataset by Dimauro and Girardi, University of Bari, hosted on IEEE DataPort) as a candidate source of raw speech audio from people with Parkinson's disease and healthy controls. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-mdvr-kclllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the "Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London" dataset (also known as MDVR-KCL, smartphone recordings of people with early and advanced Parkinson's disease and healthy controls, hosted on Zenodo) as a candidate source of raw speech audio for Parkinson's detection. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-mpowerllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the mPower dataset (also known as the Parkinson mPower mobile study from Sage Bionetworks, distributed through Synapse, whose voice activity contains smartphone recordings of a sustained "aaah") as a candidate source of voice recordings from people with Parkinson's disease and controls. FAIL if the answer does not mention mPower, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-bridge2ai-voicellm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Bridge2AI-Voice dataset (also known as B2AI-Voice, the Bridge2AI Voice adult dataset, published on PhysioNet as b2ai-voice, which includes participants with Parkinson's disease) as a candidate dataset for voice-based Parkinson's detection. Listing it with the caveat that the public PhysioNet release holds only derived features and that the raw audio needs a separate request counts as listing it. FAIL if the answer does not mention Bridge2AI-Voice, or dismisses it as having no audio at all without saying that the raw recordings can be requested.

05-access-mdvr-kcl-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that MDVR-KCL (Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London) can be downloaded openly from Zenodo without an application or approval. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0). FAIL if the answer does not mention MDVR-KCL, says it must be requested or approved before download, or gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial one).

06-access-mpower-qualified-researcherllm

PASS if the final answer states that mPower (the Parkinson mPower study data on Synapse) is not an open download but is released only to approved, qualified researchers through Synapse, and mentions at least one of the required steps: registering a Synapse account, passing the Synapse certification quiz, validating the user profile (for example with ORCID and an identity attestation letter), submitting an Intended Data Use statement, or agreeing to the conditions for use (which forbid redistribution). FAIL if the answer does not mention mPower, describes it as freely downloadable, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

07-access-bridge2ai-no-raw-audiollm

PASS if the final answer states that the Bridge2AI-Voice dataset released on PhysioNet does not contain the raw audio recordings, only derived data such as spectrograms, MFCCs or other acoustic features. Adding that raw audio requires a separate, more involved request (institutional sign-off through the project's data access committee, with data delivered via Synapse) is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention Bridge2AI-Voice, presents its PhysioNet release as providing the raw audio files, or recommends it as a source of audio without saying that the raw recordings are not in the standard release.

08-trap-uci-parkinsons-features-onlyllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the UCI Machine Learning Repository "Parkinsons" dataset (also called the Oxford Parkinson's Disease Detection Dataset by Max Little, 2007: 195 sustained-phonation recordings from 31 people, given as a CSV of 22 voice measures such as jitter, shimmer and HNR) as a source of raw audio recordings. Not mentioning it passes. Mentioning it while saying that it contains only precomputed acoustic features and no audio files passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists this UCI dataset as providing the audio recordings the user asked for, or lists it as a candidate without saying that it has no audio.

09-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known Parkinson's speech datasets with audio recordings: NeuroVoz (the Castilian Spanish corpus of parkinsonian speech on Zenodo); EWA-DB (the Slovak "Early Warning of Alzheimer" speech database, which includes people with Parkinson's disease); or the figshare dataset "Voice Samples for Patients with Parkinson's Disease and Healthy Controls" (sustained /a/ recordings made on participants' telephones, by Iyer, Prior, Virmani and colleagues). FAIL if the answer mentions none of these three.
